One reclaim admission first classifies its non-hosted rows: a child taken from `finishing` or refused by the transcript rule closes and does not keep its parent alive; a child handed to the launcher, or a child row another generation still owns, does. The hosted parent is then a rehost candidate while its `until` is future, one of those children remains live, or `ReclaimOptions.hostedInstanceLive` says its Workflow still runs or cannot determine its status. Past its `until` with no live child and a confirmed absent or terminal Workflow, it closes **`interrupted`** through the same record assembly (the reason names the deadline; filed under the metadata's thread, so the close releases the host key and the next `agent:ship` in the thread claims it). For a rehost candidate with no child, a plain-store record whose status is `completed` or `failed` (`ReclaimOptions.storedStatus`, wired to the store's `get`: the pipeline's `finish` landed in the plain store because the ledger refused its write) instead makes the row **abandoned** — the live rows go, no record is written; a live child is authoritative over that stale projection. Otherwise the candidate is **`rehost`**, handed to item 38's launcher with the row, the hosting fact, its actually-live children and the events. The provisional `interrupted` record the ship branch writes at a run's start is the normal state of a live hosted run and never abandons it — the guard reads the record's STATUS, not its existence. A store read that fails is one warning and rehosts, and an unknown Workflow status retains the hosted owner for later reconciliation. A `rehost` row puts nothing in the elsewhere map (`threadsElsewhereOf`, what the dispatcher's steer map is fed): the hosted parent occupies no thread, and a FOREIGN hosted row's entry is keyed by the ledger's key column, whose `#host` suffix no message's thread can ever match. One run's failure is reported and does not stop the others; its row stays this generation's and the next boot takes it again. A ledger without the routes or out of reach is one warning and the bot boots as before. Rows another generation still holds a current lease on (a rollout overlap) are left alone and handed back as `liveElsewhere`: the Slack adapter marks their cards (`markForeignLiveCards`) so the reconnect catch-up's orphan sweep ([slack-channel.md](slack-channel.md) item 8) asks `isLiveCard` — driven here, or live on the ledger elsewhere — and never closes a running run's card; the set is refreshed from the ledger before every catch-up scan (`refreshForeignLiveCards`, rows under a CURRENT lease held by another generation), so a generation that dies later loses its hold and its cards are swept like any orphan, and a failed refresh keeps the previous set. The cards of the runs closed here: a run taken from `finishing` gets a card that reports unconfirmed delivery — `⚠️ <agent> · ended`, `⏹`/`⛔` for a stop, `❌` for failed — before the socket opens (`closeReclaimedCards`); a saved budget outcome also reports absent or unknown authored output; an **interrupted** run's card is closed there too — `❌ <agent> · interrupted` with a note that says what to do next (`closureNote`), a ship pipeline's names the PR it had opened, if any, and the re-issue that continues it (`agent:ship` with the PR URL, or with the task when no PR existed), every other run's says to re-send the request — and a ship pipeline's note is also posted as a reply in its thread, because its work stands on GitHub with nobody driving it. The orphan sweep is the backstop for a card the reclaim could not close, no longer the only closer (it only looks two hours back, less than a pipeline's ceiling). **`POST /admin/crash`** (`src/channels/adminCrash.ts`) is the kill injection for testing a crash live: a `SWITCHBOARD_INGRESS_TOKENS` bearer with `deploy:write` — the same authorization as `deploy restart` — gets `202 {ok, generation, pid}` and the process exits hard 50 ms later (`process.exit(137)`: no drain, no handoff, no finish writes; the platform restarts the container). Not a self-SIGKILL: the bot is PID 1 in its container and the kernel drops a SIGKILL that init sends itself, so a self-SIGKILL would answer 202 and leave the run finishing on the same generation. No bearer 401, no scope 403, no token map 503, only POST. The Worker shim forwards it like any other path. **Records file by the metadata's thread, never the ledger's key column** (record 0060): `reclaimedRunRecord` and the closure the reclaim hands back (`ReclaimedClosure.threadKey`, what the interrupted notice posts by) both read `row.meta.threadKey`, so a hosted row — claimed under the host key (item 29) — closes under its conversation. **The reclaim's outcome is reported to the object** (record 0064): after the classification, `reclaimRuns` posts every taken row's word — `resume`, `restart`, `rehost` or `closed` — to `POST /plane/reclaimed` (`RunLedger.planeReclaimed`); only `closed` records an ending (`lease_lapsed`), a row that carries on records nothing, and the standing causes come back so the interrupted note RENDERS the plane's word (`closureNote` with `endingCauseWords`) and never composes one; an older state Worker without the route is one warning and today's words stand. **The object's alarm helps the sweep** (record 0064): it is set to the earliest hosting deadline, lease end or re-ask across the rows and re-armed only when that earliest changes (the owner's heartbeat, moving the lease end, moves it); at a lease end it only OFFERS the row — the row stays live and unclosed, claimable by any generation but its owner, the open effects re-pushed — and never ends a run: a lapsed lease on the owner's own row is a heartbeat that could not land, and the owner's next heartbeat refreshes an unreclaimed row. Before classifying an ordinary reclaimed row, recovery reads its stored record. A non-provisional, non-restarting `completed` or `failed` record with the same run id, channel, requester, thread and start time settles the lease through the fenced abandon path with `settleTerminal: true`. The store verifies the same saved terminal identity and archives recorded checks in private evidence before removing the live row. Ordinary abandonment still refuses recorded checks. It does not write another close, change the result or assign a new plane ending. An unavailable read, conflicting terminal identity or refused settlement keeps the row held. Provisional and restart records still use normal recovery; hosted parent classification keeps its child-liveness rules.
37. **Resuming a run — the pieces** (`src/core/runLedger/resume.ts`, `src/core/harness/pi/harness.ts`, `src/core/runRegistry.ts`, `src/core/runLedger/writeThrough.ts`; the boot step that puts them together is the next item). **The plan** (`planResume({transcript, lastStep, tools})`) is pure: no step record → `interrupted` (killed before the seed); an incomplete transcript → `interrupted` naming the gap; then item 31's `transcriptCompleteness` decides. **`resume`** — the last step's record landed: its `inFlight` calls were dispatched and their results died with the process, so every `tool_use` of the transcript's last assistant turn (which must be exactly the recorded calls, else `interrupted`) is **settled** (`settlementFor`): a tool that no longer exists → a synthetic result saying so; a call's action is `rerun` **iff** its tool is `sideEffectFree` (reads, GitHub reads, web, skills) or on the closed rerun-safe list (`RERUN_SAFE_TOOLS`: `update_status`; the `submit_*` recorders); every other known tool — `bash`, pi's `write` and `edit`, the GitHub writes, a bridged MCP tool that mutates — gets the synthetic result "restarted while this call was in flight; its effects are unknown — re-check them before re-running it" (`isError: true` in the conversation, `ok: false` on the stream). The default is the synthetic result: re-running is earned, never assumed — and on the harness nothing is re-run at all: the action decides the words of the restart note the rebuilt session ends on, `its result was lost — re-check its effects before re-running it` for a `rerun` call and `its effects are unknown` for the rest ([harness-pi.md](harness-pi.md) item 8). The turn's calls and the recorded calls must be the same set — a call on either side the other does not carry is `interrupted`. With nothing in flight, a transcript ending on a user turn (the seed, or a results turn) simply continues; the record's `turn`, `iteration` and `remainingMs` carry over and the step is `stepRecorded`. A transcript ending on a text-only assistant turn with nothing in flight is the model's final answer, and the plan is **`finish`**: the loop had ended, only the post-steps and the reply are owed, and the plan carries that turn's text as `answer` with the record's counters (no model call, no settlement). An assistant turn carrying tool calls the record does not know is `interrupted`. **`run-step-fresh`** — the next step's turns landed but its record did not: nothing of it was dispatched, so all of its calls run fresh, the step is unrecorded (`step + 1`, `iteration + 1`, `turn + 1` unless the calls are all `update_status`); a fresh step whose assistant turn has no calls is the final answer whose record never landed, the same `finish` with the counters advanced. **The run loop's finish entry** (`runLoop`, on a `finish` plan): no model loop runs; how the loop had ended is read back from the notes the previous generation published (`loopEndingOf`, the last ending note wins: a soft `stopped` note is restored onto this generation's `RunControl`, so the status is `stopped_soft` and the answer wears the soft stop's label as if this process had taken the stop; a `time_budget_exhausted`, `turn_budget_exhausted`, `stuck_loop` or `sandbox_dead` note puts the harness's write-up label on it ([harness-pi.md](harness-pi.md) item 15; the last two are the deleted native loop's kinds, on rows from before it); no note is a plain answer; a hard stop is no ending here, it leaves no answer turn), a `resumed` note says the model had already answered, and the post-steps run with the answer in hand exactly as after a live loop: the reviewed-head settle, the workspace observation, the description turn, the coding PR post-step (open-or-edit, idempotent by itself), the review post-step. A verdict a previous generation already posted (`reviewPostedBefore`: the last `review_posted` event among the replayed events) is the post-step's outcome on the record; neither the settle nor the post runs again and the reviewed head is the event's. The leftover pi is ended at the pid and root the row's facts name ([harness-pi.md](harness-pi.md) item 8), and a post-turn the run still owed is not run — there is no session to prompt ([harness-pi.md](harness-pi.md) item 14). **The harness's entry** (`PiHarnessResume`): the transcript is the conversation; the deadline is `now + remainingMs` instead of the agent's budget; a `resumed` run note says how many calls were in flight and how each was settled and whether pi was found alive; the settlements are answered, never re-run — one error `tool_result` per call in flight carrying the restart note, the user turn the rebuilt session ends on when pi is restarted from the transcript, or the answer the relay gives a surviving pi's re-ask for the same call ([harness-pi.md](harness-pi.md) item 8) — and the mirror's next step report carries that results turn. **The registry** (`create(label, meta, {id, replay})`) creates the resumed run under the ledger's run id with the events published before the restart replayed under their original `seq`s (bounded like any backlog) and the counter continuing past the highest, so the finish record and the ledger's event stream stay one contiguous record. **The write-through** (`adopt({runId, threadKey, state, lastStep, lastSeq})`) takes up a reclaimed row with no claim and no seed: the heartbeat starts at once, steps number on from the last record, appends continue past the last seq, patches merge into the state the previous generation recorded. **The run tools** ([agent-conductor.md](agent-conductor.md) item 8) settle by the same rule: `await_runs` is side-effect free and runs again — the wait picks up from the children's current ends, a child another generation finished meanwhile read from the store; `send_to_run` and `spawn_run` are mutations and get the restart result — never a second push into a child's inbox, never a second child.
38. **Resuming a run — the launch** (`src/core/boot.ts`, `src/core/resumeLaunch.ts`, `src/core/dispatcher.ts`, `src/channels/slack.ts`, `src/channels/slack/statusCard.ts`, `src/core/nullChannelIo.ts`; wired in `src/index.ts`). **The reclaim hands resumable rows back** instead of closing them: a nonterminal row from `live` or `handoff` whose transcript is whole and whose last step record item 31's rule accepts (`resume` or `run-step-fresh`) comes out of `reclaimRuns` as `resumable` — the row (ours now), the record, the transcript and the events — while everything else closes as item 36 says. **The reclaim repeats** (`startReclaimSweep`, every `LEASE_MS`): a kill lands at an arbitrary point in the lease, and the next boot arrives seconds later, so at boot the dead generation's lease is usually still current and the rule rightly leaves the row alone — without the sweep a killed run would sit orphaned until the following restart; the sweep takes it within two intervals of the lease expiring, refreshes the cards other generations hold (item 36) and launches the resumes. One pass at a time; a failing pass is a warning. **The launcher** (`launchResumes`, after `app.start()` for the boot's rows and from every sweep pass) plans each run (item 37's `planResume`, with the agent's static tools as the known tools — a bridged MCP tool is unknown and gets the not-available result) and dispatches it through the ordinary `dispatch()` with a `ResumeContext` (the row, the last step record, the plan, the events published before the restart, their highest `seq`, the repo context from the row's meta); a run whose plan is `interrupted`, whose agent this build does not know, or whose channel cannot be resumed on is closed with that reason (`closeReclaimed`); the launcher tells its caller once per run when the resume is over (`onDone`: the dispatch settled, or the run closed here), which is how the harness door stops holding for that run's bearer ([harness-pi.md](harness-pi.md) item 7). **The resumed dispatch** runs through the same admission, workspace attach, tools, post-steps and finish as a fresh run, with these differences: the ledger row is **adopted** at the admission point — before the card, the repo resolution and the attach, so the heartbeat keeps the lease through a slow resident attach — never claimed or seeded; a resume is never a follow-up: a thread that already has a run in flight (the user re-mentioned the bot after the kill) closes the reclaimed row `interrupted` with no reply and no steer, and a resumed dispatch that ends before its run starts (an unknown provider, a refusal, a gate) closes its adopted row the same way, so the sweep never relaunches a run that cannot start; the repo context is the row's, never re-resolved from the message; the attach-head guard does not run (the stored head is the run's; the settle after the loop still reconciles a moved head); the conversation is the transcript and the system prompt is the row's, verbatim; the run is created in the registry **under its own id**, with its **original start** (the row's `startedAt`, so the record, the card's elapsed time and the admission slot's — the steer ack's "N in" — span the whole run) and the earlier events replayed under their seqs — the card repainted as the first generation painted it, the row's `meta.route` (item 35) putting the `route reason: <reason>` note back on the label (a `debug` note, [routing-and-config.md](routing-and-config.md) item 28 — painted only when the request's level shows it, and no footer on the close), the router never asked again (the resumed message pins the preset by directive) and no second `route` event published — so the run page URL and the record are the same run and the record is one contiguous stream; no second `input`/`run_meta`/`context` event and no tombstone; only this generation's events are appended to the ledger (the subscription starts after the highest replayed seq); the dispatcher's state — checklist, verdict, PR description, pushed branch — comes back from the row, the verdict and description re-validated through the tools' own parsers; the runner re-enters from the plan (item 37), or, on a `finish` plan, the loop skips the model and runs the post-steps with the answer the transcript holds. The message the dispatch runs under pins `agent:`/`model:`/`effort:` from the row's meta with the original request text from its `input` event as the label — the model sees the transcript, not the message. **The channel**: a Slack run resumes on its thread (`resumeSlackIO`: the thread and requester from the row's METADATA — `resumeIoTarget` and `resumeMessage` read `meta.threadKey`, never the ledger's key column, which on a hosted row carries the `#host` suffix no channel's thread can match; record 0060) and keeps the card the previous generation posted — `status()` edits it in place instead of posting a second one, so the thread shows one card whose frames carry on (a card deleted since falls back to a fresh one); an HTTP or MCP run, whose caller is gone, resumes on a null channel (`nullChannelIO`: the reply is logged, never sent — its deliverable is the record and, for a coding run, the PR — and the run's seal says `replyOk: false` with `replyNote: "no channel to deliver to"`, because a reply nobody could receive was not delivered); any other channel closes `interrupted`. The reconnect catch-up never re-dispatches the original mention: it wore the previous generation's 👀 and is handled. Ship pipelines never resume through a dispatch (item 35): a hosted parent comes back from the reclaim as **`rehost`** (item 36) and the launcher **re-hosts** it instead — no plan, no channel handle, no agent of this build needed — the registry row is recreated under the run's id (`create(label, meta, {id, startedAt, replay})`: the row's original start, the label the row's meta kept, a fresh live token, the METADATA's thread on the identity — never the ledger's `#host` key — and the ledger's events replayed under their seqs), the ledger row is **adopted** (`adopt` with the row's state and the highest replayed seq, its `onStop`/`onFenced` wired to the recreated run's control, so the heartbeat keeps the host key from the moment of the rehost), and the write-through is subscribed after the highest replayed seq, so only what this generation publishes — the runner's later writes — appends to the ledger; the row's card, when it has one, is claimed as this process's own (`keepCardLive`, wired to the adapter's `adoptLiveCard`) — the rehost dispatches nothing, so no `status()` would register it, and an unclaimed card would be closed as interrupted by the reconnect catch-up's orphan sweep ([slack-channel.md](slack-channel.md) item 8) seconds after boot while the runner and its children keep running; the runner's later redraws edit the same message and its terminal frame closes it — and `onDone` fires at once, there being no dispatch to settle.
39. **The drain is a handoff, and a fence is a stop** (`src/index.ts`, `src/core/runLedger/writeThrough.ts`, `src/core/drain.ts`). **SIGTERM**: after the Slack socket closes, the drain marks every run a resume can continue — `LedgerRun.resumable`: its seed and seed record landed, it was adopted from a resume, or it is a hosted ship parent ([record 0060](../../decisions/0060-a-ship-pipeline-is-a-live-run-for-its-whole-life-and-runs-on-every-channel-that-can-open-a-thread.md): a row with no process of its own, which the next generation re-hosts); never a detached run or a run whose seed failed — `handoff` on the ledger in one call (`LedgerWriteThrough.handoff`, `POST /runs/handoff`), so the next generation takes them at once whatever their lease (item 31's reclaim, item 38's launcher). Those runs are **not waited for**: they keep running here until the exit, and their writes are fenced the moment the next generation reclaims them. Only the runs a resume cannot continue hold the drain, up to `DRAIN_DEADLINE_MS` as before; with none, the drain waits `HANDOFF_BUDGET_MS` (6 s) for pending history writes and reflections and exits. The handoff is the recovery for a roll that happens — a hard kill, a forced deploy — not a licence to roll: the deploy preflight still refuses while runs are in flight and fails by name past its budget ([slack-channel item 8](slack-channel.md), [release-and-deploy item 13](release-and-deploy.md)), because a resume the next generation fails (a harness refusing the rebuilt session) ends the run it was meant to save. A handoff the ledger refuses (unreachable, no routes) is a warning and the old wait. The drain-deadline abandonment writer skips the handed-off runs: their record is the ledger's. A handoff closes no row, so the plane records no ending for it (record 0064): the cause is assigned only when a row closes — by the next generation's finish or reclaim close — never by the drain that handed it on. **The finishing gate**: `finishing()` answers `ok` (reply), `fenced` (another generation owns the run — the `live → finishing` CAS was refused because the row was reclaimed, or the row is gone because the other generation already finished it) or `unavailable` (the ledger could not be asked, or the run is untracked or was detached for a reason other than a fence: the run is this process's, reply as before) — a run detached BY a fence (a heartbeat, step, append or state write refused as `fenced`) answers `fenced` from then on, so the reply and the record stay the other generation's. A handed-off run that completes before the next generation reclaims it replies itself: `handoff → finishing` is allowed for the owner (the reclaim then finds no row), so the handoff never discards a finished answer. On `fenced` nothing more reaches the thread from this process — no reply, no card close, and **no record**: the run is the other generation's now and its record is theirs to write; a partial record from here could race the real finish. **A fenced write stops the run**: a heartbeat, step, append or state write refused as `fenced` detaches the run (item 35) and, once, tells the dispatcher (`onFenced`), which hard-stops it — its next tool call would act on a run someone else is driving. `LedgerWriteThrough.liveRuns()` names the runs this generation drives (opened or adopted, not finished, not detached); `/healthz` is unchanged.
40. **The durable inbox** ([thread-admission item 5](thread-admission.md) has the admission side). `run_inbox` holds the follow-ups steered into a live run, one row per push with a per-run `seq` (the message with its attachments when it fits the route's 512 KiB body — [thread-admission item 5](thread-admission.md) has the cap and the drop note; a staged file's reference — name, size, type, `url_private`, message id, [execution.md](execution.md) item 20 — is metadata and rides the base row, so it survives the restart even when the row drops its inline attachments, and reads back as the same reference); `pushInbox` is never fenced (a steer arrives on whichever container is up). It returns `not-live` for an absent live row and `invalid-message` when custody cannot be verified; `readInbox(runId, afterSeq)` (`POST /runs/inbox/read`, any generation) is the same slice on demand — the resume's re-read at adopt time, so a push that landed after the reclaim's snapshot is found; the write-through answers it empty with a warning when the ledger cannot be asked. Each step record carries `inboxConsumedSeq`, the highest inbox seq among the follow-ups the run had folded in when the record was written (the runner keeps the counter; a resume starts it at the last record's), and `inboxDeferredSeqs`, the seqs at or below it the run was handed and its model has not read — pi's harness names every follow-up still queued or steered-but-unechoed there, since one drain can hand pi an ordinary steer and the plane's provider-up row and pi reads the reissue prompt first; the reclaim hands the resume exactly the items past the cursor plus the deferred ones (`unreadInbox`, shared by both ledgers; the Worker refuses a deferred list that is not positive integers) — `ReclaimedRun.inbox`, carried through `ResumableRun` and `ResumeContext` — and finish preserves original inbox rows rather than crediting delivery or cursor advancement as native consumption. The write-through's `pushInbox(runId, message)` answers the sequence after a valid append, or undefined with a warning only for explicit target absence. Other rejections and storage errors propagate without delivery or fresh-routing fallback. A null ledger still represents disabled durability. **The plane writes rows here too** (record 0064): the checkpoint steer — one fixed sentence for a coding run whose in-flight call is past its declared bound (or the no-bound line) or whose round pushed no head past `noPushMinutes` (default 15; `PLANE.noPushMinutes`), at most once per run per round per cause and the same sentence at most once per round — and the reissue steer for a run parked on its provider. A provider-up decision writes the typed sender-`plane` message as a `run_inbox` row and offers a `steer` effect carrying that row's per-run sequence inside the same transaction (the dedupe rows ride `plane_reservations`, kind `steer`/`park`, and the park plus any open live steer go at the run's seal). The state Worker pushes the committed effect through `/plane/effects`; before the local registry can accept it, the push path atomically verifies the open effect and authoritative owner generation on the ledger and renews that owner's lease, so reclaim cannot cross the synchronous inbox put and a stale registry row left during handoff or reclaim overlap defers. The owning generation puts the already-durable message into its in-memory inbox, where the sequence deduplicates push, heartbeat and reclaim delivery. It acknowledges `done` after that put, `skipped` when the sequence was already accepted, and `deferred` while draining or when another generation owns the run; `done` and `skipped` close a steer only under the same authoritative owner fence. A deferred or stale-acknowledged offer rides later owner heartbeats, and the durable row remains the reclaim fallback. `/runs/live` rows are what the admission map is built from (`ThreadsElsewhere`), replaced whole on every reclaim pass; `LiveElsewhere` names the thread, start and agent for it.
41. **One registry on every read surface** ([live-view item 17](live-view.md)). With the ledger on, `RunsService` takes it as a read source: the ledger's live rows whose run is NOT in this process's registry — live under another generation (a rollout overlap), or reclaimed here and not yet launched; never a run this process is attaching, whose registry row dates from its reservation (item 42) — are **live rows** on every surface, built from the row's meta and start and the events the ledger holds (`readEvents`: count and activity), with `ownerGen` naming the generation driving them and never a capability token (the page token is the other generation's). `listRuns` lists them under `all` and `active` (never `finished`, never on a cursor page — live rows sort ahead of any cursor) and suppresses their store rows whatever was asked (item 27); `liveElsewhere(visibleTo)` is that slice alone, for the default index. `getRun` (with the events on a messages read), `getRunEvents` (a seq page) and `getRunFriction` (a live diagnosis) answer for such a row after the registry and before the store; `stopRun` asks the ledger (`requestStop`), which the owning generation reads on its next heartbeat, and answers `stopping`. A run in both the registry and the ledger is the registry's (once). The viewer's predicate applies to ledger rows as to any row. A ledger that cannot be read is one warning per call and the registry (and the store) alone — never a failed read. A failed live-row listing sets `ledgerUnavailable: true` on `listRuns` for every status and cursor page: a short fallback listing is not proof of cross-generation completeness, including for tombstone suppression. This is separate from `storeUnavailable`, since readable history remains available. An empty successful ledger listing, a later successful retry, or a deployment without a ledger carries no failure marker. The web surface admits such a run tokenless ([live-view item 16](live-view.md)): its page token is the other generation's, so the viewer's attribute decision is its only gate; the page renders live with a running server clock and time allocation, and its explicit EventSource follow tracks the ledger by sequence until a terminal receipt; default tokenless replay reads remain finite snapshots for work folds, the friction route answers the live diagnosis, and the tokenless stop route stops it through the ledger for a viewer holding `runs:write` (the tokenless view route remains operator-only; the command surface adds requester cancellation under [authorization item 5a](authorization.md)). One ledger listing serves every service read within `LEDGER_LIST_TTL_MS` (2 s) — a page view's three reads, or a history read of a finished run, cost one ledger call — and the rows' events are read in parallel; a failed listing is not kept. The remote event stream refreshes without overlapping reads; unavailable or denied reads close transport without reporting a terminal outcome. The owner and sweep retain all execution authority. A deferred checkpoint event marks a ledger view as `recoveryPending: true` with activity `paused for storage recovery`, including when its pause handoff was not confirmed. A later recorded resume clears this projected hold. This read-only fact does not resume the producer or change its ownership.

42. **A run is on the ledger from before its workspace attach, and a kill there restarts it** ([thread-admission item 5](thread-admission.md)). Until this item the claim (item 35) came after the attach, so a run was carded and in flight for the whole attach — a minute on a resident's mirror mutex, minutes on a cold clone — with nothing on the ledger: a kill there lost it (no resume, no record, the card left for the orphan sweep; seen live on a review whose resident attach waited out the mirror mutex). Now the dispatcher **reserves** the row right before the attach (`LedgerWriteThrough.reserve`, `POST /runs/claim` with `phase: attaching`): the run's id (minted from the registry at admission, `RunRegistry.mintId`, and handed back to `create`), thread, start, card, the run meta, and the **request** in `meta.request` — the message in the durable inbox row's shape (`durableInboxMessage`: text with its directives, sender, link, thread, arrival time, the attachments when they fit `DURABLE_INBOX_MAX_BYTES`, else `attachmentsDropped`) — with an empty prompt; the heartbeat runs from here, so a long attach keeps the lease. **The registry row is created immediately after the reserve (`registry.create` with the minted id, the card's start and the composed label), so the run is one row on every surface from admission on: the runs index lists it with its label and capability link, the run page serves it, the card carries the live link through the attach, the HTTP ingress answers with its id, and a stop relayed during the attach latches in its control for the run loop's first step. (Before this the row came after the attach, and the index showed the reservation meanwhile as a labelless item-41 ledger row with a tokenless link that 404'd once the run was promoted.) Its stream is live from registration too: `bindRun` after the reservation (the spans before it — the root, the ack card, the repo resolution — backfilled; the reservation, the attach and the resident's grafted steps streamed as they happen), then `input`, `run_meta` and `context` published right after, before the attach — so the run page shows the request and the setup steps while the workspace is still being attached, and the index's event count moves. The request stays the record's first **content** event; the setup spans ahead of it are head material (`isHeadMaterial`: `request`, `slack.receive`, `dispatch.*`), so the protected head runs unbroken from the first event through the request. The tombstone (item 27) still waits for the run loop. Every advertised run keeps its identity through setup: a shared finalizer closes a pre-model exit under that same id, through the ledger's atomic finish when tracked or the history store when untracked (item 63). The claim once the prompt exists is the same request as before and **promotes** the row in place (`decideClaimWrite`: `insert` for a fresh thread, `promote` for the owner's attaching row — prompt, tools, card and state land, phase `live`, identity and start untouched — `refresh` for an owner's re-reserve, `keep` for any re-claim on a row past attaching), through the same tracked run (`OpenRunRequest.reservation`), so the seed and every later write are exactly item 35's. `phaseTransition` allows `attaching → live` and `attaching → finishing` (a dispatch that fails before its prompt); never `attaching → handoff` — an attaching run has nothing to resume from, so the drain waits for it as it always did. A ship run is never reserved: it claims its own row without a seed (item 35) and closes `interrupted` at a reclaim as before. **Reclaim** (item 36) leaves an expired `attaching` row in that phase (`reclaimPhase`) and hands it to the launcher as a `RestartRun` — row, unconsumed inbox, no transcript. **The launcher** (`launchResumes`) reads the request back (`messageFromInbox`) and dispatches it again as the message it was — directives included, so the same agent and model resolve, and for a routed row the row's `meta.route` (item 35) carried into the route stage, so the same preset resolves with no second router call and the fresh record gets its `route` event ([routing-and-config item 21](routing-and-config.md)) — with a `RestartContext {row, inbox}`: the dispatcher claims the thread in-process with the row's original start, reserves the row again (the owner's idempotent re-claim, for the heartbeat), folds the carried inbox plus a re-read of anything later into the run's inbox (item 40's rule, no second ack), keeps the row's card (`resumeSlackIO` with the row's `card.ts`) and id (`registry.create` with `id` and `startedAt`), and runs the request from scratch — attach, prompt, promotion, steps, finish — so the record carries the original start and both the request and the follow-ups as `input` events. A restart onto a thread that has a newer run in flight (the user re-mentioned after the kill) closes the reserved row `interrupted` with no reply. A reservation that meets the row of a run this process is still closing — a restart from that run's request, or the fresh turn for its unconsumed follow-ups, both dispatched from its finally — waits for that finish and claims again, rather than running untracked (item 54 has the rule and its bound). **A fenced reservation** — the lease lapsed during the attach and another generation reclaimed the row — is told through `onFenced`; the dispatch stops at the attach's end, releases the workspace and says nothing: the run is theirs to restart, and a promotion that finds its own run under another generation answers the same way (`claim` → `fenced`, nothing seeded). **An ordinary run's promotion whose claim goes untracked** — the retries spent or the route missing while the reserved row stood — **abandons** the row from `open` itself (never `close`, which only stops the heartbeat and leaves the attaching row for the reclaim sweep to restart while the untracked original still runs), says why through `OpenRunRequest.onUntracked`, and the dispatcher publishes the `ledger_untracked` note and marks the card `untracked by the ledger` from `claimRun`, as the reserve-time path does (item 54); the abandon owns the write-through's dead-reservation bookkeeping, so an abandon that itself fails leaves the run known there and the thread's next claim abandons the row again (item 54's safety net). **Every fresh run that reaches `registry.create` gets one setup finalizer before its link is exposed.** The finalizer stays active through promotion, seed and working-state commit. An ask-once refusal, moved-head gate refusal, failed attach or other pre-model exit after registration finishes the same run ID through its tracked reservation or untracked history store, retains its finished row and record, and releases the thread before a queued follow-up claims. A stop keeps its stop status. A fence leaves the row to its new owner without a model call, reply or finish from the old generation. **Only a reservation that never reached `registry.create`** has no setup finalizer or advertised link; the outer finally abandons that reservation before settling the thread. A fenced or untracked reservation has no owned row to abandon. The in-process steer's durable copy (item 40) lands from the reserve on; only a steer in the moment between admission and the reservation (the ack card, the repo resolution) rides in memory alone. **The same finally is a second net** for a registry run a branch of the dispatch opened itself and left unfinished — `runShipBranch` opens its own row and finishes it on every non-hosted exit ([agent-ship.md](agent-ship.md) item 16), but a row a branch left `running` has no runner behind it: a stop is a request to the runner, so it can never take, and the shutdown drain waits on the row to its deadline. Once the branch has returned or thrown, any run bound to this dispatch — the trace's bound run (`RequestTrace.runId`, set at `bindRun`), the thread slot's (`LiveThread.runId`) — that is still unfinished is finished `failed`, with a log line naming the run and the branch, before the thread is settled. The one deliberate survivor is the hosted parent of a completed hand-off ([record 0060](../../decisions/0060-a-ship-pipeline-is-a-live-run-for-its-whole-life-and-runs-on-every-channel-that-can-open-a-thread.md)): the branch names it (`ShipBranchEnd.hostedLive`) and the plan runner's `finish` ends it, so the net leaves it live.

43. **A narrow lookup: the submitted PR description for a head** ([reading-diff.md](reading-diff.md) item 7). `findSubmittedPrDescription(store, { repo, pr, headSha }, limit = SUBMITTED_LOOKUP_LIMIT = 10)` (`src/core/reviewDescription.ts`) is the one read of the store the dispatcher makes for a running run: `store.list({ limit, visibleTo: { kind: "repos-in", repos: [repo] } })` — the repo the review is authorized for, newest first — then `store.get` on each row, scanning its events newest first for a `review_artifact` `pr_description` with `origin: "submitted"` whose `repo`, `pr` and `headSha` all equal the key; the first match wins, its `fromRunId` kept (a review run's copy names the coding run). At most `limit` records are read; no new store method, filter or column — the record's `repo` is the only index used, and a store that has no such record answers "none" after `limit` reads. `RunDeps.runStore` is the seam (`NullRunStore` in a process without run history: the lookup finds nothing and the review parses the body). A store failure surfaces to the caller, which degrades to the parsed body.
44. **Finished reviews retain abridging attempts; artifact events use one append path.** The review abridger can update `readingDiffAbridge` metadata on an existing finished review ([reading-diff.md](reading-diff.md) items 5–6). `ReviewAbridger.saveAttempt` re-reads the record and writes the latest attempt through `put`. The attempt keeps its start, last update, state, up to 32 redacted progress lines of at most 512 characters each, and a final error of at most 4,000 characters. These updates survive restart. Full internal reads and explicit review status retain them; metadata-only summaries and listings omit them. Attempt updates do not add events or advance event counters. They are separate from the artifact append below, and neither path creates a missing record. The abridged reading diff is produced AFTER the review, so `appendReviewArtifact` (`src/core/reviewAbridge.ts`) re-reads the record, drops a previous meat artifact (a `--force` replaces, never accumulates), stamps the new event with the next `seq` after every event the record ever held (the replaced one included, so an `afterSeq` cursor that saw the old one sees the new), bumps `eventCount` for a new artifact (not for a replacement), refits the byte budget with `fitRecordToBudget` — whose per-event cap leaves a `review_artifact` alone: its `diff` is capped by its producer above `MAX_EVENT_BYTES` by design and its `summary` is one line — and `put`s the whole record: the same-id upsert every store already implements (`rewritten: true`, the DO rewrites the event rows in one transaction). It refuses by name when the record is gone (`the run's record is gone — nothing to append to`), when the artifact would not survive the budget, or when the store answers `stored: false` (the record fell outside retention in its own write); nothing is ever created by the rewrite. Neither attempt updates nor artifact appends emit another metrics point: the finished run's emission latch is already set, so `emits` answers false ([run-metrics.md](run-metrics.md) item 2).
45. **The `profile` field** ([record 0026](../../decisions/0026-capability-profiles-and-request-routing.md); [routing-and-config.md](routing-and-config.md) items 2 and 4). `RunRecord.profile?: { preset, machine, identity, minutes, boundedBy? }` is the effective profile the run was admitted with — the preset's name, the machine class its tools executed on, the identity it acted as, the wall-clock minutes it ran on and, when a boundary clipped that budget below the preset's own, the scope whose boundary did (`defaults | channel | user | directive`) — so a reader can tell a clipped budget from a declared one and a reclaimed run keeps the budget it was admitted with. Written by the one record assembly (`assembleRunRecord`, `profile` under the preset's name) at the tombstone and the finish, and by the reclaim from the ledger row's `meta.profile` (`LiveRunMeta.profile`, the same shape without the preset — the row carries `agent`); the drain deadline's tombstone of a run whose registry row predates profiles carries none. `isRunRecord` checks it structurally when present — a known class and identity, a positive numeric budget, a known clip scope, a string preset — and a record without one (written before the field existed) is accepted as before. The seed a fresh run claims with (item 35) carries the effective budget, so the step record's `remainingMs` a resume runs on (item 37) is the clipped one, never re-derived from a preset.

46. **The `parentRunId` field** ([agent-conductor.md](agent-conductor.md); [routing-and-config.md](routing-and-config.md) item 20). `RunRecord.parentRunId?: string` names the run that spawned this one or its thread — set by the dispatcher on a child started through `spawnChild()` (`DispatchOptions.parent.runId`), on a run a person's reply starts in a thread that spawn opened (the lineage stage, `src/core/dispatch/lineage.ts`; [agent-conductor.md](agent-conductor.md) item 10: the same parent at depth 1, no clock), and on nothing else — on every surface a run has: the registry row's `RunMeta.parentRunId` (so `RunSummary`, the index feed and `RunsService`'s views carry it, live and persisted alike), the ledger row's `LiveRunMeta.parentRunId` (so a reclaimed child's record still names its parent), the tombstone, the finish record and the drain's interrupted record. `isRunRecord` accepts a value of the run-id shape (`RUN_ID_PATTERN`) and refuses anything else; a record with no parent carries no key, and every run a person or a schedule started has none. It is what a listing draws a tree from and what the fan-out cap counts (a parent's live children in the registry); the index and the label are unchanged.

47. **`run-finished-<runId>` rides the state Worker's finish** ([http-ingress.md](http-ingress.md) item 9; [record 0029](../../decisions/0029-durable-objects-store-workflows-schedule.md)). Every terminal record commits through `RunHistoryDO.finish` — the finish after the reply (item 33), the reclaim's close of an unresumable run (item 36), the admission's close of a superseded resume or restart (items 38 and 42) — so the handler is the one place a coordinator's child reports its end: after the transaction commits, and only for a record carrying `parentInstanceId` (item 48), the object sends that Workflow instance one event of type `run-finished-<runId>` — the run id under a prefix in the platform's alphabet (letters, digits, `-`, `_`; a space, a colon or a dot is refused as `workflow.invalid_event_type`, a refused send being a lost event the parent recovers from only at its next `read-record`) — through its `SHIP_COORDINATOR` binding (`sendRunFinished`, `src/core/coordinator/contract.ts`) with the payload `{ runId, status, finishedAt, parentInstanceId }`. The type carries the run id so each `waitForEvent` matches its own child and a duplicate is buffered harmlessly; the parent confirms every event through `read-record` before it acts, so the payload carries ids and a status, nothing more. The send never touches the commit: a fenced finish sends nothing; an engine that refuses (the instance ended, or is unknown) is swallowed, named in the answer's `event` (`sent | none | no-binding | failed`) and in one warn line; a Worker without the binding commits as before and answers `no-binding`. The binding is optional on the state Worker on purpose: it is a cross-script binding to the bot shim's `ShipCoordinator`, the state Worker deploys before the bot, and a binding to a class that has never deployed fails the state Worker's own deploy — so the class ships first (the shim's own same-script binding, `deploy/cloudflare/coordinator.ts`) and the state Worker's binding followed one release later — its template names the bot's `ShipCoordinator` across scripts by the bot's script name (`{{bot.script}}`, the Workflow named `<bot script>-ship-coordinator` as the shim names it), so a state Worker deployed without the binding still commits and answers `no-binding`. Records that reach the object through `put` alone also send the event when they are a child's END outside the ledger's finish — the run loop's `interrupted` close of a superseded or reclaimed run, the pi harness's typed restart closing the old run, a resume abandoning a lost workspace — so the parent's wait settles at once instead of walking out the child's budget: the send fires for a stored record carrying `parentInstanceId` whose `finishedAt` is past its `startedAt`, which excludes the start tombstone (`finishedAt` = `startedAt` there), and the parent still confirms by `read-record`, so a duplicate send is harmless. A tombstone at a run's start sends nothing, and a lost send still falls back to `read-record` at the wait's next chunk ([http-ingress.md](http-ingress.md) item 9). The object also speaks at a claim (record 0064): a claim whose meta names `restartOf` under a coordinator (`LiveRunMeta.restartOf`, set by the restart-from-request dispatch) sends the parent instance one `child-resumed-<runId>` through the same binding, best effort — the plane's twin beside the bot's own announcement (item 47a); a duplicate is consumed and re-armed by the driver's wait, harmless — and a Worker without the binding claims as before.

47a. **A deploy roll under a coordinator child is said twice: a typed event on the child's record, and its Workflow twin beside `run-finished-<runId>`** ([agent-ship.md](agent-ship.md) item 15; issue 1364 part 2). A child interrupted or resumed by a roll — the bot's own restart resuming the run, or the resident's container replaced under it — leaves the fact on its own record and tells its waiting parent, so the plan runner ends the round with the child's own reason at once instead of walking out the child's budget, and never reads a survived roll as a lost round. **The record events.** `child_interrupted` (`{ parentInstanceId, reason }`) is published straight to the registry when a coordinator child closes `interrupted` for a restart from its request: by `abandonLostWorkspace` when a resumed child's workspace cannot be re-attached (item 54, appended to the closing row's record past the highest replayed seq beside the `resumed` note), and by the run loop's interruption (a replaced container whose relaunch was refused, another harness's row — item 54's mid-run outcome), the reason the note's own words. `child_resumed` (`{ parentInstanceId, summary }`) is published by the dispatcher when a resumed coordinator child's workspace re-attaches and the run carries on under its record, tag and budget — and by the run loop when a coordinator child's relaunch succeeds in the replacement container (item 54's mid-run resume, the resident roll survived): the typed event beside the harness's `sandbox_restarted` note, with no Workflow twin from there — a resumed child settles nothing, the parent's wait keeps waiting on the finish. Both ride only runs whose dispatch carries the coordinator tag (item 48); every other run publishes neither. **The Workflow twin.** The reattach path sends the parent instance `child-interrupted-<runId>` or `child-resumed-<runId>` (`sendChildSignal`, the same alphabet and best-effort rule as `sendRunFinished`: a refusal is answered, never thrown — a lost send costs the wait a chunk, not the round) through the process's `WorkflowSender` (the shim's event relay, [http-ingress.md](http-ingress.md) item 12); the run loop's interruption sends nothing itself — its terminal record's `put` already sends `run-finished-<runId>` (item 47). **The driver's wait** ([agent-ship.md](agent-ship.md) item 15): each wait chunk arms three `waitForEvent`s under durable names — the finish under the step's own name, the interruption under `<step>/interrupted`, the resume under `<step>/resumed[/n]` — the finish or the interruption settles the wait as the event outcome (confirmed by `read-record` before the machine acts, as every wait is), a resumed event is consumed and re-armed under the next name and settles nothing (a resumed child keeps the wait), and the chunk times out only once the finish and interruption waits both have. **The restarting ending** (record 0064): the close a restart follows carries `restarting: true` and `restartUntil` on its record (`RunRecord.restarting` / `restartUntil`, stamped from the injected close clock by `abandonLostWorkspace` through `closeResumedRow`), the deadline one central `RESTART_CLAIM_GRACE_MS` after `finishedAt` — one whole coordinator wait chunk. `read-record` searches for the same instance-and-key successor first, so a live or finished successor claimed before or after the deadline always wins as `restartedAs`; only with none does the restarting close decide the answer: one millisecond before the deadline it is still running, at or after it is the original `interrupted` ending with `restarting` suppressed from the projection and the cause read from its existing events, never `wall_clock_cap`. A process that survives still takes the eager path: `recordRestartDeath` immediately rewrites the close without `restarting` or `restartUntil` and appends `restart_died`. A legacy restarting record without `restartUntil` retains the old still-running behavior; `normalizeStored` drops the restarting projection for a deadline before its close or beyond the central grace, failing closed without making a successor invisible. The plane's `child-resumed-<runId>` from the successor's `restartOf` claim (item 47) lands beside the bot's own, and a runner that hears it twice re-arms its wait and loses nothing.

48. **The coordinator's tag: `parentInstanceId` and `idempotencyKey`** ([http-ingress.md](http-ingress.md) item 9). A run a coordinator spawned carries two required identity fields on every row it has, plus the admitted cost cap when one exists, set by the dispatcher from `DispatchOptions.coordinator` and by nothing else: `parentInstanceId`, the Workflow instance it is a child of (the platform's id alphabet, at most 100 characters, `INSTANCE_ID_PATTERN`), and `idempotencyKey`, the key its spawn carried (`<parentInstanceId>:<step>`, the step any name without a colon — `<unit>/<round>/<kind>` for the plan runner; `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_PATTERN`). The tag (`CoordinatorTag`) also carries `base`, an optional field that is NOT a record field, the branch the child's pull request targets — the parent record's `base` (item 49), set by the spawn when the instance knows one — read by the child's PR post-step alone ([pr-description.md](pr-description.md) item 5), an instruction to the run like `DispatchOptions.contract` — but one the claim publishes as a typed event so it survives the spawning process (item 48a); `coordinatorFields` spreads the two identity fields and optional `costCapUsd`, leaving `base` out; the cap survives on rows and records. The identity and optional cap ride where `parentRunId` (item 46) rides: the registry row's `RunMeta` (so `RunSummary`, the index feed and `RunsService`'s views carry them — live here, live on another generation's ledger row, and persisted), the ledger row's `LiveRunMeta` — checked at the claim (a malformed key, instance id or cap is 400, and so is either identity field alone) and stored there, so a retried spawn finds its child before any record exists and a reclaimed child's record still names its instance — the tombstone, the finish record, the drain's interrupted record and the reclaim's close (`assembleRunRecord`'s `coordinator`, the one assembly). A refused claim on a thread names the live run's key beside its id, agent and start (`decideClaim`; the in-memory ledger and the object agree), so a coordinator's spawn can tell its own child from a busy thread without a second read. The tag is both fields or neither: `isRunRecord` accepts the two by shape together, refuses a malformed one and refuses either alone, and the claim's parser holds the same line; a run no coordinator spawned carries neither key.

48a. **The tag survives a bot roll: admission persists it, restart and resume read it back, and the coordinator store is the second guard.** `DispatchOptions.coordinator` lives only in the spawning process's memory, so a coordinator's child re-admitted after a bot death would reach its PR post-step with no tag and treat the [unit](../vocabulary.md#unit) branch as its own base. Three layers close that hole. (a) Admission of a fresh coordinator child persists one `coordinator_tag` event before registration acknowledgement — `{ parentInstanceId, unit?, base?, issuedFindingIds? }`, with the exact issued IDs bound only for a findings child, the unit read off the idempotency key's step (`unitOfIdempotencyKey`, the head of the step before its first `/`) — mirrored onto the ledger row like every event; a resume republishes nothing, an attaching restart replays the original stream without duplicating its tag. (b) A resume without a tag in its options rebuilds one (`carriedCoordinatorTag`): the two record fields from the row's meta, the base and issued IDs from the row's `coordinator_tag` event; a row written before the event existed yields the tag with no base or IDs and restores its admitted cost cap from row meta, and a run no coordinator spawned yields none. (c) The run loop resolves the base once, before the harness session opens — the tag's, else `instance.base` read from the coordinator instance store by `parentInstanceId` (item 49), for a coding PR run only — and both the gate's push rules ([harness-pi.md](harness-pi.md) item 7: the recovered base is the protected branch, the unit branch the run's own) and the PR post-step read that one answer; when a coding child still has no base — no event, no instance — the PR post-step opens nothing: with a description in hand it publishes `pr_not_opened` saying the plan's base was lost across a roll and replies with the compare URL, and it never falls through to the binding ref or the repository's default branch ([agent-ship.md](agent-ship.md) item 4).

49. **The parent ship record** (`CoordinatorInstance`, `src/core/coordinator/contract.ts`; the seam `CoordinatorInstanceStore`, `src/core/coordinator/instanceStore.ts`). What the bot writes at a coordinator instance's creation and the spawn route reads the requester, channel and thread from, so no step ever takes an actor from its caller: `{ id, kind: "ship", userId, userName?, authenticatedAs?, postedBy?, channelId, channelName?, threadKey, sourceUrl?, generatedTask?, generatedTaskSource?, repo, branch, base?, createdAt, plan?, caps?, card?, runId?, label?, attempt? }` — the instance's identity and surfaces plus a first-generated-unit admission checkpoint: `generatedTask` backs up the admitted task when the first instance write precedes its unit row; `generatedTaskSource` retains the original admission run and source URL across re-issues, while the unit row (item 50) owns subsequent unit state; `plan` the plan the instance runs (its id, the file's name, and its path in the repository), `caps` the pipeline's caps as the profile gate clipped them (the rounds cap and the wall clock per unit), `card` the status card in the requesting thread the bot redraws from the coordinator's round events (`{ channel, ts }`, `StatusHandle.handle`), `runId` the id the bot writes the parent's record under when the instance ends and `label` the card's label — the instance's own two surfaces; `attempt` (an integer from 2) which re-issue of the plan the instance runs ([agent-ship.md](agent-ship.md) item 16: a later attempt lives under `plan-<plan-id>-<attempt>` and reruns the units not merged), absent for the first; beyond that first admission backup, unit state is the unit's row (item 50). One seam, two implementations ([record 0001](../../decisions/0001-seams-with-two-implementations.md)): the state Worker's `coordinator_instances` table on the run-history object — `POST /runs/coordinator/put {storeKey, instance}` → `{ ok: true }`, idempotent for the same record and `409 { ok: false, reason: "exists" }` for a different one under a taken id, `400` for a record the shared validator (`isCoordinatorInstance`) refuses; `POST /runs/coordinator/replace {storeKey, instance}` → `{ ok: true }` replaces only valid draft unit rows with no execution or publication facts, in the same transaction. A retained task claim, recovery journal, legacy ending, wake decision or unknown unit field refuses as `{ ok: false, reason: "exists" }` without changing the saved owner. Replacement is for an unstarted attempt whose Workflow creation was confirmed absent by the shim; `POST /runs/coordinator/get {storeKey, id}` → `{ instance | null }`, `400` for an id outside the platform's alphabet; the same bearer and size fence as every ledger route — behind `WorkerCoordinatorInstanceStore`, which throws on an answer it cannot read rather than guessing (a spawn on a guess would be a spawn nobody asked for); and the in-memory double. `NullCoordinatorInstanceStore` is the store of a process without a Worker-backed run history (no history, a file store, a missing bearer — `buildCoordinatorInstanceStore` follows `buildRunLedger`'s rule): it knows no instance and refuses a put as `unavailable`, so every coordinator route then refuses by name.

50. **The coordinator's unit rows** (`CoordinatorUnit`, `src/core/coordinator/contract.ts`; the same seam and store). One row per unit of the plan an instance runs — a task string is a plan of one unit, `task` — keyed by `(instanceId, unit)`: `{ instanceId, unit, slug, title?, branch, dependsOn, threadKey?, sourceUrl?, reviewThread?, issue?, pr?, resume?, record?, lastPush?, publication?, segments?, idle?, wakes?, rounds, ending?, startedAt? }` — the unit's branch (`plan/<plan-id>/<unit-slug>`, or the task's ship branch) and the units it waits on, written at the instance's creation; its thread once the runner opens it (a task's is the requesting thread from the start) and, on a row a bot wrote before record 0055, its review thread (`reviewThread: { threadKey, sourceUrl? }`, retired: a new row never gets one and every child runs in the unit's thread, [agent-ship.md](agent-ship.md) item 5; a row that carries one keeps its review rounds there), its board issue when one titled by the unit id exists, its pull request, a `resume` (the open pull request of ship's own the requester named, its head and url — written by the hand-off on a task's row, read by the driver so the unit opens at its review round; [agent-ship.md](agent-ship.md) item 10), the renewals it spent (`segments: { index, from?, runId?, at }[]`, one per segment the grant opened after the first, written by `unit-end` on a `continued` ending before that segment runs and never twice for one index, so a reclaimed runner finds the row; [decision 0046](../../decisions/0046-a-budget-is-a-lease-carved-from-its-parent-and-one-module-proves-the-leases-fit.md)), the round boundaries the coordinator reported (the `ship_round` vocabulary, oldest first) and how it ended (the kind, the thread's report, when) as the runner reaches them — or that it idles ([record 0051](../../decisions/0051-a-thread-has-one-owner-for-its-life-a-message-is-one-event-in-a-chosen-mode-and-a-pipeline-idles-instead-of-ending.md); [agent-ship.md](agent-ship.md) item 8): `idle { why, at, renewalsLeft, from?, runId?, spendUsd, handoff?, wakes }`, written by `unit-end` on an `idle` ending in place of `ending` so the unit stays unfinished — `why` the old kind (at most `IDLE_WHY_MAX` characters, refused longer at the route), the continuation facts beside it (an idled `review_pending` names the pending head as `from` and the body's `headSha` still lands as `lastPush`), `wakes` zero at the write; every future `merge_ready` ending likewise writes its final reviewed full head to `lastPush`; a normal reply may repair a legacy `merge_ready` row only from one full head jointly attested by that same unit's completed coding record (its final head or an exact-unit-branch durable `pushed[]` head) and posted approving review record, with exact parent/idempotency/requester/repository/pull-request binding — a same-thread standalone run is not unit evidence — after fresh exact requester, open same-repository pull request, head/base ref, live head and sole-owner verification, by one conditional atomic transition that requires the exact unchanged legacy row, reserves sole ownership, and replaces it with both `lastPush` and the complete existing-PR `publication` binding; invalid, ambiguous or stale evidence leaves the row unchanged; indexed answers live beside it as `wakes: Record<waitId, UnitWakeAnswer>` so replay reads the same `segment`, `answered`, `stopped` or `expired` decision, and the state Worker writes that answer, its segment row and consumed event marks atomically; a later real `ending` drops the idle from the row — with the report still posted to the unit's thread and the parent card's line reading `idle · <why>` — so a unit's thread, branch, pull request and ending are one row a person can read, and a runner instance resumes at the first unit without an ending by reading the rows, not its memory. On the object: `coordinator_units` beside the instance table; `POST /runs/coordinator/units/put {storeKey, units}` upserts each row whole (a replace keeps the row's place; an empty list, more than 200 rows, or a row `isCoordinatorUnit` refuses is `400`); `POST /runs/coordinator/units/claim-legacy-continuation {storeKey, expected, recovered}` compares and replaces one unit row in a single transaction (`409 stale` when the exact expected JSON no longer stands); `POST /runs/coordinator/units/list {storeKey, instanceId}` → `{ units }` in the order first written (the plan's), `400` for an id outside the platform's alphabet, `[]` for an unknown instance; the same bearer as every ledger route. The in-memory double keeps insertion order the same way; the null store refuses a unit read rather than certifying an empty list, and refuses a put as `unavailable`. The state Worker's test pool binds the bot's script as a stub Worker carrying a `ShipCoordinator`, so the cross-script binding resolves in workerd and a test that needs a Worker without the binding installs its absence on the live object.
51. **A run on the pi harness is one more row of the same shape** ([harness-pi.md](harness-pi.md) item 8). The harness's mirror writes the ledger's step records as the native loop did — the assistant turn with its calls in flight before the results, the results and any steer's text as the next step's user turn, the echoed prompt skipped as the seed — so a pi run's transcript is `ChatMessage`s and `planResume` reads its row unchanged; pi's compaction entry is one more row of the run's range (item 53; [session-log.md](session-log.md) item 6), written as its own step with nothing in flight. The row's `state.harness` carries the facts a resume needs beyond the transcript — `{ pid, logOffset, root, sessionFile, bearerHash, container }`, written when pi starts, the offset moved to the boundary after each row the mirror writes ([harness-pi.md](harness-pi.md) item 8 says what it names and why never a turn's end) — and the resume launch hands the plan to the harness like any run's, which either re-attaches to the container's pi at the recorded offset (the calls in flight settled on the relay first, so the extension's re-ask for one reads the restart result; the continue queued as a steer, since a pi inside a call refuses a plain prompt) or restarts pi on a session file rebuilt from the transcript (the same result written as those calls' tool results) — on either path every call in flight is answered with the restart result of item 37 and never re-run. Three `run_note` kinds are pi's alone (`compacted`, `harness_error`, `tool_refused`; [run-visibility.md](run-visibility.md) item 1); no event type is.
52. **The `seed` field** ([routing-and-config.md](routing-and-config.md) item 20; [agent-conductor.md](agent-conductor.md) item 3). `RunRecord.seed?: RunSeed` names where the run's conversation started — `channel`, its own thread's history, as for every run a person, a schedule or a coordinator started; `parent`, a spawned child seeded from admitted structured evidence at the spawn (`DispatchOptions.parentContext`); or `session`, a follow-up on the pi harness seeded from the tail of its own session's log ([session-log.md](session-log.md) item 9), whose `session.seedFrom` names the log index that tail began at — set by the dispatcher from the seed source it resolved and by nothing else, on every surface a run has: the registry row's `RunMeta.seed` (so `RunSummary` and the index feed carry it, and the drain deadline's interrupted record reads it off the row), the ledger row's `LiveRunMeta.seed` (so a reclaimed child's record still says so), the tombstone and the finish record — the one assembly (`assembleRunRecord`) carries it. A child's `context` events are its seed turns when it has them, so the record shows what the model saw. `isRunRecord` accepts `channel`, `parent` and `session` and refuses every other value; a record written before the field existed carries no key and reads as it always did.
53. **A run is a range of its session's log** ([session-log.md](session-log.md); [record 0035](../../decisions/0035-a-session-log-outlives-its-runs-compaction-is-a-pointer.md)). The transcript of a run with a conversation of its own lives in `SessionLogDO`, one object per working session (`SESSION_LOGS` binding, migration `v9`), which holds every run of that session's rows in order and is kept when a run finishes. Ordinary work uses `<threadKey>:<agent>`; a unit lane uses `<instance>:<unit>:coding` or `<instance>:<unit>:review` across rounds. The shared `<threadKey>:@thread` control log carries connector and report turns separately from those fenced execution rows ([session-log.md](session-log.md) item 13). The write-through reads the log's tail before the claim and the row's `meta.session` names the range the run occupies — `{ key, threadSession?, seedFrom, request, range: { from } }`, where a fresh claim's `threadSession` names the exact shared thread key and an adopted legacy claim retains its absence: the seed's rows are appended from `seedFrom` (a thread's first run of an agent starts at 0; the next starts where the last left off), the request is the seed's last user turn, and every step's turns land at `seedFrom` plus their index in the run's own conversation, under the same generation fence and `(idx, part)` upsert as item 32 and before the tools run as item 35. The finished record carries `RunRecord.session` with the range closed at the last turn written, or `range: "broken"` when a refused or twice-failed write detached the run (item 35) — the log then ends short of what the model saw, and the record says so; a record without `session` (written before the log, or a run without a conversation) validates and reads as before. A reclaimed row resumes from its log (`transcriptSource`; item 31) and keeps appending there; a row claimed before the log existed resumes from and finishes on its own `RunTranscriptDO`. Retention: `runs.session_key` names the session a finished record was a range of and the object's `sessions` table lists every session a claim or a record named; the enabled 6-hourly sweep, after it trims the runs, drops each session object no remaining run row or retained child context reference names and whose thread has no live run — owner row first, then the rows — and its registry row (session-log item 7). Storage per object is bounded by `RetentionPolicy.sessionLogMaxBytes` (session-log item 5). **The tool events name their rows.** A `tool_call` carries `logIndex`, the log row of the assistant turn it rode in, and a `tool_result` the row of the user turn its batch's results make: the harness asks the mirror which of the run's own rows each is (`PiMirror.rowOfCalls` — the last assistant turn the ledger holds, written here or met again on a re-attach; `rowOfResults` — the next row, where pending results and a steer's text go first) and the ledger run for that row's place in the log (`LedgerRun.logIndexOf`: `seedFrom` plus the index, the one arithmetic every seed and step write uses), so a stamp and the row the step wrote cannot disagree. A run without a session, a record from before the field and a call the mirror cannot place (a re-attach whose transcript ended on a compaction) carry none, and every reader takes such a record as before. The `assistant` event is not stamped: the bridge emits it as the turn ends, before the mirror writes the row, and its prose shares that row with the turn's calls, which place it. This is how a search hit's turn finds its step ([session-log.md](session-log.md) item 11).
54. **The row records where the run's workspace is, and a resume re-attaches there; a legacy lost workspace restarts the run, while a pilot writer pauses on its original binding** ([resident-repos.md](resident-repos.md) items 24 and 66; [harness-pi.md](harness-pi.md) item 8). Until this item a resumed dispatch (item 38) attached its workspace exactly as a fresh run does: the resident's attach treated the run's own dirty tree as disposable and recreated it under the run's still-living pi, or raced the run's in-flight install and failed, and the factory's fallback then moved the run onto a cold sandbox with a `cold_sandbox` note, where the pi probe looked in the wrong container and read a living pi as dead. **The binding.** The claim (item 35) writes `state.binding` beside the harness facts: `{ backend, workspace?, user?, container? }` (`WorkspaceBinding`, from `workspaceBindingFor(selection, machine)`: the selection's backend, and the known checkout from either a resident attach or sandbox seed, plus a resident pool user and container; nothing for the `none` class, which has no workspace). **The re-attach.** A resume reads it back (`carriedWorkspaceBinding`: the state's binding, or on a row written before it a binding derived from `meta.selection` and `meta.workspace`) and hands it to the attach as `ExecutorContext.reattach`, and the factory then provisions nothing: a recorded resident is probed and asked to keep the thread's tree as it stands (`reuse: true`, resident-repos item 66), and its answer must name the run's own worktree and pool user; a recorded per-thread backend re-makes the per-thread executor for the thread with no resident probe, so a run that started cold is never moved onto the resident; the recorded backend is the only one consulted. A successful re-attach writes the binding back complete (a row from before the container was recorded learns it). Explicit `residentId` and `placementId` metadata also stays with that binding. Outside pilot preservation, a failed reattachment with any explicit resident address, including malformed metadata or a contradictory reply, fails setup without restarting on an address-free binding ([exact resident address](resident-repos.md#exact-resident-address)). **The restart for ordinary legacy runs.** A resident found `restoring` is first waited through, never refused at once ([execution.md](execution.md) item 27's held `/await-restore` request, on the re-attach too — issue 1364 part 1): the run's worktree lives on that resident or nowhere, and its restore ends on its own, so the hold — bounded by the restore ceiling clipped to the run's lease — re-attaches the same worktree and the run carries on under its id, record, tag and budget instead of restarting from its request. Past that, anything short of the run's own tree (a refusal, an unreachable or unserviceable resident, a needs-ref, another tree) is a `WorkspaceReattachRefusedError`; for an opted-in pilot writer `attachWorkspace` holds the same ledger row and recorded binding for later recovery, while an ordinary legacy binding without resident address metadata answers `reattach_refused`, and the dispatcher (`abandonLostWorkspace`, `refuse("workspace_lost")`) publishes a `resumed` note naming the refusal, closes the card, closes the adopted row `interrupted` with that note appended to its record past the highest replayed seq (an operator's stop that ended the re-attach's wait is not a refusal: the request ends `stopped` and the row closes with the stop's status, `stopped_hard` or `stopped_soft`, never `interrupted` — [execution.md](execution.md) item 9), and, once the outer finally has freed the thread, dispatches the row's request again in the thread under the SAME run id (`prepareRestartTurn`: the request as `meta.request` carried it with a fresh receipt and its own root, the follow-ups the resumed run never consumed appended as a fresh turn appends them), provisioned as a fresh run is, on a fresh card, and naming the run it restarts (`DispatchOptions.restartOf`) so thread admission never steers the request into the row it closed. **The restart keeps the run's identity** (issue 1880; one identity, as a renewed lease opens a new segment under record 0046): the closing run's registry row is read before the wind-down discards or seals it (`carriedRunIdentity`: its retained events, its capability token, its original start) and rides the dispatch as `DispatchOptions.restartCarried`; `registerRun` then reuses `restartOf` as the run's id, replays the carried events under their seqs, keeps the token (`CreateOptions.token`) and the start — so the page a person opened for the run, every posted capability link (the card's, a unit thread's) and the ledger row stay valid across the replacement, the runs index and the plane's tables count one run (the registry re-creates under the same id, replacing the finished row; the ledger's live row and the stored record share the id and every list dedupes on it; the run store's put is an upsert, so the run's final record replaces the interim `interrupted` one), and the record reads as one run whose transcript carries the replacement as ONE `resumed` note in user words ("container replaced, resumed from the request" for a replaced container; "workspace lost, resumed from the request" for a lost workspace), published between the replayed segment and the restart's own turn. Only when the closing run's registry row is already gone does the restart run under a fresh id — a page that moved, never a request that vanished. **The reservation and the finish in flight.** The restart — and the fresh turn for a closed run's unconsumed follow-ups ([thread-admission item 4](thread-admission.md)), dispatched from the same finally — is dispatched right after the closed run's finish was handed to the history writer, so its reservation (item 42) can reach the ledger ahead of that write — for a restart that keeps the run's id, as the owner's idempotent re-claim of the very row the landing finish is about to delete; for the fresh turn, answered `thread-live` for the row it is about to release. A claim under a run id whose own finish is named in `landing` therefore awaits that finish FIRST, before its first ledger call, and then claims — the row gone, a fresh row for the run's new segment is inserted; a finish that failed for good leaves the run's own row standing and the idempotent re-claim keeps it, the run riding its row tracked (no `ledger_untracked` note: the row is its own). The write-through keeps every finish this process is landing named by run id (`landing`, from the sink's first `put` to the outcome: landed, refused, or failed for good), and a claim answered `thread-live` for a run named there claims **once more** after awaiting that finish — its own promise, an awaited event, no timer of the write-through's own; what bounds it is the finish's attempt sequence, every ledger call of an attempt aborting at the store client's `AbortSignal.timeout` (`RUN_STORE_TIMEOUT_MS`; a finish is the state flush, the append flush and the `finish`, so two or three calls), the writer's fixed backoff between its attempts, and the caller's **final word**: a failed `put` settles nothing by itself — the sink never re-derives a retry policy — and the name settles as failed when the caller says `RecordSink.abandoned` (the history writer at its give-up: a missing route, a permanent failure, the attempts spent; a one-shot closer, `closeResumedRow`/`closeRestartRow`, right after its own failed put), so no waiter outlives its caller — the word is **required** on every sink (a store, keeping nothing in flight, answers no-op), so a sink that keeps something in flight cannot forget it and park its waiters; the writer says it from its defensive catch too (a rejection outside its stop rules — the injected sleep, a hook — is still its give-up), and a one-shot closer says it through `putOnce`, which puts a record once and says the word on a failure before rethrowing. **A dead reservation of this generation.** A `thread-live` naming a run this generation reserved and never promoted, with no tracked run driving it and nothing landing for it, is this process's own reservation that never started and whose abandon did not reach the ledger (item 42's one unretried call): the claim abandons it again from there and claims once more, so no dispatch ordering in the finally has to be right;
