an abandon that keeps failing leaves the run untracked with that why. `reserve` answers the outcome itself (`ReserveOutcome`: `tracked` with the run, `untracked` with the why, `fenced`, or `off` for a process without a ledger), and the reservation stage puts the why on `Reservation.untrackedWhy`. A `thread-live` naming a run whose finish **landed** in this process most recently (`landed`, the newest `LANDED_MAX` = 256 finishes, oldest out — an entry covers only the moment after its finish, and a resident roll finishes every live run within seconds, so the newest few hundred hold far more than that window needs; an older one is a stale row) is the moment between the answer and the row's release, and is claimed once more at once; a row named neither there nor in `landing` is another run's for real and is untracked in one claim, as item 35 has always had it. The second answer is the last: a finish that failed or was refused leaves the row standing, and the run goes on untracked, by name, never refused (an untracked run still runs and replies, item 35; a refused request would be lost) — **and says so on its own record, whichever way the reservation ended untracked**: the write-through hands the why back on every untracked exit — the row of a run this process closed still stood after its finish settled (`did not land (<error>)`, `the ledger refused its finish`, or another run's row holding the thread now), the row is another run's whose finish is not in flight here, the state Worker has no run-ledger routes, the claim kept failing — and the dispatcher publishes it from one place as a `ledger_untracked` note (head material, like `cold_sandbox`) right after the reservation, the card's label carrying `untracked by the ledger` — the bot log's warning is not the only witness. The wait sits inside the reservation, so the in-memory-only steer window (item 42: from admission until the reserve resolves) spans it too; nothing is lost unless the process dies inside it. Before this rule the reservation was refused and the restart ran untracked — no row to hand off, resume or reclaim, and the store holding only its start tombstone, which any reader without this process's registry (the CLI's `runs get`) shows as a finished `interrupted` run while the run turns on — and carrying the coordinator tag the interrupted run had (`DispatchOptions.coordinator`, item 48a: the run loop's interruption hands over the tag it ran under, a refused re-attach rebuilds it from the row's meta and its `coordinator_tag` event), so a coordinator's child restarts as the same instance's child — its unit branch stays its own push target and the plan's base stays the branch its pull request targets, where a restart without the tag read the unit branch as the base, refused the child's push and could not open its pull request ([thread-admission.md](thread-admission.md) item 5); a row whose request cannot be read ends there, the note and the card saying so. The transcript is not continued on a fresh tree: the work it describes is gone with the tree, and a restart from the request is the honest continuation. Never a silent migration: a resumed run reaches a sandbox only if its row says it ran on one. **The same step mid-run.** The re-attach is one step the dispatch-time attach and a run in flight share (`reattachWorkspace`: the binding handed to the factory under the `dispatch.workspace.attach` span, the factory's refusal read by name), so a process relaunched in a replacement container can re-attach the run's binding without the dispatch-time gate — the ask-once question, the card and the reply stay the dispatch-time attach's (`attachWorkspace`). Outside a pilot writer's recorded preservation policy, an interruption mid-run is the run loop's own outcome, never an exception the dispatcher catches: a container replaced under the run ([harness-pi.md](harness-pi.md) item 16), a row another harness wrote ([harness.md](harness.md) item 7) — the loop finishes the run `interrupted`, ends the process, releases the workspace, closes the card `🔁`, and answers `RunInterrupted` (`kind: "interrupted"`; the card's `reason`; the request's `refusal` by name; the record's `note`; and `restart`, the request to run again with the run it restarts), and the dispatcher reads the refusal as the request's outcome and dispatches the restart exactly as it dispatches this item's, once the thread is free, once. Under a living bot a container replaced under the run is first a relaunch, not an interruption ([harness.md](harness.md) item 6): the loop re-attaches the run's binding through this item's `reattachWorkspace` — the same round back, the resident asked to keep the tree as it stands — before the process is started again in the replacement, and only an ordinary run's refusal by name (the refusal `workspace_lost`) or the relaunch ceiling closes the run this way; a pilot writer's re-attach refusal leaves its row and workspace binding for the next generation.
55. **A quoted conversation is on the record** ([record 0037](../../decisions/0037-a-linked-thread-is-quoted-not-joined.md)). Right after `input`, the provision stage publishes one `reference` event per conversation the request pointed at and the references step quoted: the block as the model saw it (`text`, redacted, under the per-event byte budget by the step's own 32 KB cap), the classifier's fresh `channelName`, the `channelId`, the message count and the permalink (`url`). The finished record derives `references: { url, channelId, messages }[]` from those events at assembly (`referencesOfEvents`, the way `pr` comes from `pr_opened`), absent when the run quoted nothing, so a pull request a steered run opened traces back to the text that steered it; `isRunRecord` accepts a record with or without it. The event is head material (`isHeadMaterial`): published contiguously after `input`, it survives the backlog trim and the record budget that drop the oldest events first, so the audit trail is never the first thing to go; a captured events file replayed through the analyzer keeps it too (`isRunEvent`). The friction analyzer counts the event as a side fact; the card's activity line names it `referenced thread quoted`; the `input` event stays the request text alone.
56. **The record carries what the run cost** ([costs.md](costs.md), cost by user). `usage` — `{ turns, byModel: { <provider>/<model>: { turns, inputTokens, outputTokens, cacheReadTokens, cacheWriteTokens } } }` — is summed at the one assembly (`assembleRunRecord` → `usageOfEvents`) from every `model.turn` span end the run published, BEFORE the byte budget can cut a middle event, so every record written since carries it, zero turns included; a turn without a model attr counts under `unknown`. The Worker store keeps it in its own column (`usage_json`, NULL for a record written before the field) and answers `POST /runs/usage {storeKey, sinceMs, untilMs}` → `{ runs: [{ id, userId, userName?, parentRunId?, threadKey, channelId, agent?, startedAt, finishedAt, usage? }], parents: { <run id>: { userId, userName? } }, pending, earliestFinishedAt?, retentionDays }`: one row per kept run that finished in the range, oldest finish first, plus the identity of every parent a child names that is outside the batch and known to the store. The arithmetic is the bot's (`aggregateUsage`, `reportOfUsageRows`, `RunStore.usage`): a child billed to its parent's requester (a parent in the batch, in `parents`, or the child's own id when no parent is known), the runs summed into **usage cells** — one per (billed requester, thread, channel, agent, UTC day of finish), `wallMs` the summed finish − start, a record naming no agent under `unknown` — the cube every cost dimension is a sum over ([costs.md](costs.md) items 10–10a), so a snapshot holds cells for allocation and individual coordinator-child points for the Ship spend trend, each with its durable unit key, finish time, recorded usage and admitted cap when known. Other runs have no per-run point. A row without `usage_json` is priced there and then from its stored `model.turn` events and written back — at most 200 a call, the rest answered without `usage` and counted in `pending` — so the history heals as it is read, with no operator step; a run without turns is written back as the zero usage so it is never re-read. The range may span at most 366 days and must be non-empty; a local store (`InMemoryRunStore`, `FileRunStore`) answers the same cells over its records, pricing a usage-less record from its events, so it never reports pending; the null store answers an empty report.
57. **The failure by name** ([harness-pi.md](harness-pi.md) item 6; [session-log.md](session-log.md) item 9). `RunRecord.failure?: { kind: "policy_refusal" | "provider_transient" | "model_stream_incomplete" | "sandbox_fleet_busy" | "restart_unclaimed" | "harness_ending_unconfirmed" | "runtime_shutdown_unconfirmed" }` (`RunFailure`, `RUN_FAILURE_KINDS`) names a failure or an unconfirmed runtime ending when the failure has a name a reader acts on: `policy_refusal` — the model provider refused the run's call under its usage policy, read from the stop reason its wire names and never from the explanation's words; `provider_transient` — verified provider transport failure spent the lease-backed retry window (a gateway 5xx or HTML page, overload or reset); `model_stream_incomplete` — pi ended an answer stream without a complete answer and spent its local retry lease, with no provider failure established ([execution.md](execution.md) item 29 and [harness-pi.md](harness-pi.md) item 6). Ship reads either model retryable kind to re-run a round-0 child only when its workspace had nothing to checkpoint; a pre-model sandbox fleet refusal may recheck the original PR head and retry review inside the same unit ([agent-ship.md](agent-ship.md) items 8 and 9). `harness_ending_unconfirmed` retains an original producer whose end did not acknowledge exit. `runtime_shutdown_unconfirmed` retains a killed run whose producer or required cleanup did not confirm shutdown. These records refuse destructive removal; a finished stream is not a shutdown receipt. The run loop sets model failures from the harness's typed errors (`ModelPolicyRefusedError`, `ModelTransientFailureError`, `ModelStreamIncompleteError`) and hands it to the one assembly (`assembleRunRecord`) through the finish record; the shared setup finalizer sets sandbox_fleet_busy only from the executor's typed spent-wait facts before any model turn; `restart_unclaimed` is the run store's own: its sweep closes a `restarting` record whose claim deadline passed with no successor holding the id as `interrupted` with that kind ([run-metrics.md](run-metrics.md) item 3a); the tombstone and the drain deadline's interrupted record carry none — a run that has not failed has no failure. The runs service carries it on the view of a persisted run (`RunView.failure`), which is how the thread read finds the request rows the next seed leaves out. `isRunRecord` accepts the named kinds and refuses any other shape; a record without the field — a run that did not fail, one that failed for a reason without a name, one written before the field existed — validates and reads as before.

58. **Listings across runs: a unit's, a parent's, a session's, a pull request's** ([agent-ship.md](agent-ship.md) items 17 and 18, [agent-conductor.md](agent-conductor.md) item 11, [session-log.md](session-log.md) item 11; `src/core/runsService.ts`, `src/core/unitRuns.ts`, `src/core/findingsLedger.ts`). `RunListOptions.parentRunId` narrows a listing to the runs naming that parent (item 46) on every store — `selectListItems` for the in-memory and file stores; a `parent_run_id` column on the Worker's `runs` table, indexed in the page order and written beside every row at `put`, a table from before the column gaining it at construction filled once from each record's own `summary_json` so a child stored earlier still lists under its parent (`RunHistoryDO.migrateRunsTable`, idempotent, the one method every column migration lives in) — and the `/runs/list` body validates it as a run id. `RunListOptions.pr` (`{ repo, number }`) narrows a listing to the runs that name one pull request the same way (`namesPullRequest` in `src/core/runRecord.ts`: the row's `repo` is the pull request's and `pullRequestNumberOf(row)` — the coding post-step's `pr.number`, else a posted review's `reviewPost.target.number`, nothing for a skipped post — is its number): `selectListItems` for the in-memory and file stores; a `pr_number` column on the Worker's `runs` table, indexed with `repo` in the page order (`runs_pr`), written beside every row at `put` and filled once at construction from `summary_json` by the same rule for a table from before the column (`migrateRunsTable`) — and the `/runs/list` body validates it as an `owner/name` slug and a positive integer, its two bound parameters taken from the same headroom `visibleTo` fits under only when asked. `RunsService` adds four reads over its one listing, each under `visibleTo` ([run-visibility.md](run-visibility.md) item 9): `listUnitRuns(unitKey, visibleTo)` — the unit's row from the coordinator's store (item 50; `RunsServiceDeps.units`, the instance for the requester's identity when the reader sees no run), both threads' runs through the `threadKey` listing, cut by `unitRunsOf` — answering `UnitRunsView = { unit, instanceId, threads, rounds, runs: (RunView & { round, thread })[] }` or `not_found`; `listChildren(parentRunId, visibleTo)` — the `parentRunId` listing in start order; `searchSession(key, query, limit, visibleTo)` — the ledger's session search (`RunsServiceDeps.sessions`: the same ledger object as `ledger` in the bot; the CLI builds it and the coordinator's store from the run-history config beside its run store, `buildCoreCommands`) answering `SessionSearchView = { session, hits: [{ turn, role?, snippet, runId?, gap? }], gaps }`, each hit's `runId` the finished or admitted active run of the session whose exact recorded or live range (item 53; [session-log.md](session-log.md) item 11) holds the turn and `gap` the newest gap marker the hits straddle below it. `listFindings({ repo, number }, visibleTo)` — the `pr` listing joined with the unit rows that name the pull request, handed to `ledgerOf` ([agent-ship.md](agent-ship.md) item 18) — answering `FindingsLedgerView` or `not_found`. `runs unit`, `runs children`, `runs search`, `runs findings` and `runs list --parent` are their commands ([command-registry.md](command-registry.md)).

59. **The intake receipt store** ([record 0058](../../decisions/0058-a-thread-reply-is-read-before-it-is-answered-intake-decides-whether-the-bot-was-addressed.md); [routing-and-config.md](routing-and-config.md) item 27). `RunLedger.recordIntake(key, receipt)` writes one row to `intake_receipts` on the `RunHistoryDO`, keyed by the message (`<channel>:<ts>`), as insert-if-absent inside `transactionSync` — the first writer's row stands, this and every later write answers `{inserted, stored}` with the stored row (`decideIntakeInsert`, the shared decision), so one unmentioned thread reply is decided once across processes. The row (`IntakeReceipt`, `src/core/runLedger/types.ts` — the shape the intake seam re-exports) holds `verdict`, `reason`, `source`, optional typed `providerFailure`, `mode`, `model`, `gen`, `threadKey`, `decidedAt`. `readIntake(key)` answers the row or none; `listIntake({threadKey?, since?})` answers a thread's rows or the rows since an instant, oldest first — what the live false-silence ratio reads. The routes `POST /runs/intake {storeKey, key, receipt, windowMs?}`, `POST /runs/intake/read {storeKey, key}` and `POST /runs/intake/list {storeKey, threadKey?, since?}` carry `storeKey` like every run route, refuse a malformed key, receipt (`isIntakeReceipt`) or filter as 400 and no bearer as 401. Provider-failure delivery is a second durable row: `POST /runs/intake/delivery/claim {storeKey, key, poster, claimedAt}` atomically grants one poster the receipt-keyed right to publish unless delivery already succeeded or an unexpired claimant stands; `POST /runs/intake/delivery/finish {storeKey, key, poster, delivered}` closes the owner's success forever or releases its rejected post. `INTAKE_DELIVERY_CLAIM_MS` bounds recovery when a process dies while owning an unfinished claim, and a stale/non-owner finish changes nothing. When scheduled cleanup is enabled, rows are pruned on the object's alarm after the larger of 24 hours and the writer's reconnect catch-up window plus the drain deadline (`intakeReceiptRetentionMs` in `src/core/budgets.ts`; `prune_after` stamped at the insert from the write's `windowMs`, the client's `catchUpWindowMs`), so a catch-up that runs after the longest allowed drain still reads the verdict instead of deciding again. While cleanup is paused, expired receipts remain readable and the same keys continue to deduplicate. The write-through retries a lost response as the claim does (`RETRY_MS`): the retry READS the same row — the insert may have landed — and a row carrying this write's `gen` and `decidedAt` answers `inserted: true`, another writer's answers what it stored, none at all is inserted once more; a missing route, a permanent refusal or a failing retry answers undefined with one warning — the caller degrades rather than falling silent (record 0058). The in-memory ledger implements the receipt reads/writes and the same atomic delivery claim with a failure toggle (`intakeFailure`) so tests can make a write or a read throw on demand. The Slack gate writes and reads these receipts on both the live and catch-up paths; a provider-failure receipt preserves the cause so neither path reconstructs it from the rendered reason.

60. **The operator's shadow decision beside the routed request** ([record 0057](../../decisions/0057-the-operator-is-the-one-door-a-model-binds-every-chat-input-and-deterministic-code-authorizes-fences-and-executes.md); [routing-and-config.md](routing-and-config.md) item 29). Under `routing.operator: shadow` or `on` every admitted chat event's decision is one `operator` event on the run that carries the request — the agent run's record beside its `route` event, the inline command run a typed line becomes, or the live run a reply was folded into — published like the `route` event (`OperatorEventFields`) and projected onto the record as `RunRecord.operator` (`operatorOfEvents`, the `routeOfEvents` pattern). The event holds the mode, the outcome (`binds`, `question`, `refusal` or `non_decision` — the structured seam's floor, [record 0067](../../decisions/0067-one-seam-for-a-structured-answer-a-violation-is-re-asked-with-the-violation-named-and-the-callers-declared-floor-holds-never-a-refusal-shown-to-the-person.md)), the reason, the seam's `attempts` (what each answer violated, or that it was accepted), the bound lines redacted and cut like the receipt (`ROUTE_RECEIPT_CAP`) — never the message text — a question's proposed line (`proposal`, cut the same way: what the next turn's "yes" binds, [routing-and-config.md](routing-and-config.md) item 29), the intake gate's verdict when the gate was present, and the turn's latency and output tokens for the replay's medians ([load-harness.md](load-harness.md) item 17). Under `on` a decision that starts no run records on a door record of its own (`recordOperatorDecision`: agent `door`, status `failed` for `non_decision` and `completed` for a question or refusal, the redacted request as `input` and the decision as its one `operator` event); a bind that runs carries the event on its command run. It follows the record's retention like every event; a record without the field validates and reads as before; nothing runs from it under `shadow`.

61. **A child's durable context manifest.** `RunRecord.childHandoff` carries the bounded `ChildHandoff` manifest from the parent: source identities, session ranges, typed source receipts, tool-result references, notes and attachment references. `isRunRecord` delegates its validation to `isChildHandoff`; malformed manifests are rejected on persisted reads. The manifest is separate from the coding outcome's existing `handoff`. A new session claim records `RunSession.threadSession`, the canonical `<threadKey>:@thread` migration marker, including when a work unit uses its own session key. The marker must be a valid session key for that record's exact thread; legacy records may omit it. The operator's repository provenance includes `context` for a canonical repository inferred from notes, repository briefs or other admitted context.

62. **Original source evidence survives finish.** `RunRecord.sourceReads` archives the canonical source-action state, including original response bodies and pending or unknown actions, under that run's exact id, requester, agent, channel and thread. `sourceReadState` validates this owner against the record rather than trusting the archive's owner field. `contextDependencies` carries the bounded canonical dependency closure; an origin naming this run must match its identity. Finish and reclaim retain these fields and `childHandoff` before the live row disappears. Full internal record reads retain source bodies; listing rows exclude `sourceReads`, and every outward `RunsService` projection removes the archive, raw child context and dependency envelope, including full-record reads that include events. A malformed archive or dependency envelope remains invalid, never a source-free legacy record. Retention uses dependency references without exposing bodies in its summaries.

61. **Exact owner evidence for resident cleanup.** The bearer-protected `POST /runs/preservation-owner {storeKey, runId}` reads one run ID. A live owner takes precedence over a stored summary, including a provisional summary. Only a non-provisional terminal record is returned as terminal; an unknown or malformed record never authorizes resident deletion. This read does not use the retention-filtered list or summary route. The resident uses the fixed store key and the existing state Worker credential.

63. **An advertised run survives setup failure.** A fresh run reserves before its registry id or Live run link is published. A pre-model exit finishes that same id, through the tracked ledger reservation or the history store when untracked; it never creates a second door record. An untracked reservation omits the Live run link because its history write is not yet proven. A stop keeps its stop status. A capacity ending has a typed failure and zero turns, so a review with no verdict or GitHub post is distinguishable from one that reached the PR.

## Request failure context

The [shared request contract](tracing.md#request-failure-context) supplies a diagnostic trace reference. Run history retains that reference independently of the event backlog.

The producer attaches a typed request-failure context. The registry projects that generic field once in its shared append path, without inspecting model diagnostics. `failureContext` retains the last observed trace reference with unverified provenance outside the event backlog; null records a later failure whose origin is unavailable. Replay uses the same projection. Terminal record assembly preserves it through storage, and can reconstruct retained evidence during a reclaim write. Run metadata reads return that field without copying or reading events. Private event projection preserves only the validated structural reference. The reference grants no source access, recovery or replay authority. Existing live-ledger discovery and development-store retention costs remain separate from this bounded projection and the keyed production summary read.

## Validation criteria

| Criterion | Proof |
|---|---|
| The shared request failure is private, bound to the edge's own trace, and retained through the installed adapter, event eviction and history reload with one summary read; incoming trace parents confer no identity and the failure confers no retry authority. | `[unit]` `deploy/cloudflare/containerStartContract.test.ts::public request failure context::*` |
| Failure context remains bounded and unverified, live metadata does not copy events, replay uses the same projection, and a later failure cannot inherit an older origin. A failed reporter never exposes decoder prose. | `[unit]` `src/core/requestFailure.test.ts::shared request failure context::*` |
| A reclaimed write can derive a retained failure context; malformed stored references are refused and a newer unknown origin remains null. The production summary row persists the reference without retained events. | `[unit]` `src/core/dispatch/record.test.ts::assembleRunRecord — the handoff on the record::reconstructs retained request failure context on write and rejects malformed stored references`, `deploy/cloudflare-memory/runLedger.test.ts::request failure context in stored metadata::persists a bounded failure context in the keyed summary without retained event rows` |
| An optional HTTP read-scope preview returns the exact requested channel, requester and repository with permission from the same compiled list predicate and the channel-directory machine stamp, even when history is empty. It creates no run or ending fact, grants no access, and requires the paired requester/repository options and an HTTP channel, refusing partial or unsupported identifiers. Lists without this option retain their existing shape; revoked command grants cannot obtain a preview. | `[unit]` `src/core/commands/runs.test.ts::runs.list::proves exact HTTP read scope from the existing policy even when history is empty`; `::refuses incomplete or unsupported HTTP scope requests`; `::a revoked command grant cannot obtain a scope proof` |
| Hard stop fences the exact original and terminates only proven owned work. A stable native replacement epoch may acknowledge runtime retirement while retaining unknown historic effects and original journal bytes; UID reuse by a successor never authorizes a signal. Without original native review target receipts, missing bindings may request positive discovery only from the recorded Resident backend, never infer an absent workspace or guess a target. Unknown runtime or terminal acknowledgments retain the fence and cannot claim shutdown. | `[unit]` `src/core/runLedger/originalPromotionHold.test.ts::original promotion hold diagnosis::hard stop cancels an unconfirmed original without confirming its source or retaining an active run`; `deploy/cloudflare-memory/runLedger.test.ts::original workspace durability in real SQLite::hard stop cancellation closes a real SQLite unconfirmed original without releasing its source`; `::a lost cancellation terminal acknowledgment reconciles only a committed record…`; `src/execution/runStop.test.ts::run runtime stop::*`; `src/execution/sandboxLifecycleReader.test.ts::sandbox run cancellation::*`; `deploy/cloudflare-resident/workspaceSettlement.test.ts::resident run cancellation::*`; `src/core/runsService.test.ts::RunsService.stopRun::keeps a refused cancellation fenced and seals the same ticket after an acknowledged retirement`; `src/core/commands/runs.test.ts::runs.stop::names a known shutdown refusal without claiming the stop was unrecorded or suggesting replay`; `deploy/cloudflare-resident/workspaceSettlement.test.ts::resident cancellation admission::*`; `src/core/runLedger/originalPromotionHold.test.ts::original promotion hold diagnosis::unsupported local fallback retains ordinary hard stop and canonical finish`; `::cancelled resident work retains exact terminal settlement ownership`; `deploy/cloudflare-memory/runLedger.test.ts::original workspace durability in real SQLite::cancelled SQLite resident ownership remains readable and can be acknowledged without a VM`; `src/channels/commandHttp.test.ts::createCommandHttpHandler — hard stop after restart::*` |
| Hard cancellation of a deferred review checkpoint preserves both possible outcomes of a lost state-write reply, retains the original ticket and deferred event, and requires an independent runtime acknowledgment before sealing `stopped_hard`. It retains a verdict already saved by a committed checkpoint without creating a new verdict or publication receipt, and rejects late original writes after the seal. | `[unit]` `src/core/runsService.test.ts::RunsService.stopRun::hard stop preserves deferred review checkpoint outcomes without posting: committed=%s` |

| Large authored Unicode text and PDF data use bounded same-owner write chunks and project one exact legacy logical part; missing, changed, reordered, duplicate or malformed internal content remains a typed required hold | `[unit]` `src/core/runLedger/transcript.test.ts::internal bounded Memory transcript::*`; `deploy/cloudflare-memory/runLedger.test.ts::original ingress SQLite owner::stores bounded Unicode text and PDF chunks on the original owner and returns an exact legacy transcript` |
| Oversized canonical compaction holds before any upload; an ordinary summary keeps the original compaction DTO | `[unit]` `src/core/runLedgerWorker.test.ts::WorkerRunLedger::holds oversized canonical compaction before uploading any content` |
| Unknown chunk acknowledgement keeps its exact write witness and publishes no logical row; an older Memory codec is refused before content upload | `[unit]` `src/core/runLedgerWorker.test.ts::WorkerRunLedger::keeps a lost chunk acknowledgement uncertain and never publishes its logical row` |
| Required transcript failures cannot become channel or alternate-agent history, while ordinary legacy missing-log behavior remains unchanged | `[unit]` `src/core/dispatch/seed.test.ts::sessionSeedFor — the seed read from the ledger, with the notepad::holds unreadable required transcript bytes instead of falling back to channel history`; `src/core/dispatch/operator.test.ts::operatorThreadTail carries each turn's actor off the assembled transcript::holds a required transcript failure instead of falling back to another agent log`; `src/core/dispatch/operatorTail.test.ts::operator source-aware tail::holds required transcript failure before substituting source-aware empty history` |
| A checkpoint write ACK must match its original run/generation/key/before-hash/revision/inputs. An optional closed local session boundary is checked without changing wire bytes; InMemory rejects a mismatch before recovery effects. | `[unit]` `src/core/runLedgerWorker.test.ts::checkpoint acknowledgment original binding::*`; `src/core/runLedger/inMemory.test.ts::local original checkpoint boundary before effects::*` |
| A HERE hosted hard stop requires confirmed pipeline admission-stop, including an exact committed readback after a lost reply, and preserves the human stop intent but closes a tracked canonical run only through its own writer's reconciliation, sink and known terminal ledger ACK. Missing, ambiguous, foreign, handed-off, unstarted, off or fallback evidence does not grant closure. Explicit registry-only and remote stop paths retain their existing behavior. | `[unit]` `src/core/runsService.test.ts::RunsService.stopRun — a hosted parent: soft refused, hard seals (record 0060)::*` |

| Criterion | Proof |
|---|---|
| Malformed possibly-committed live-state and context-checkpoint write replies retain the exact original wire request as an uncertain effect. Non-JSON replies remain uncertain; known canonical refusal codes keep their precise negative result. No ambiguous reply authorizes success or mutation replay. | `[unit]` `src/core/runLedgerWorker.test.ts::status and checkpoint mutation acknowledgement custody::*` |

| Criterion | Proof |
|---|---|
| The first original harness facts may carry a closed launch policy. Canonical policy survives omitted harness/policy updates and legitimate lifecycle changes; malformed or changed policy refuses. A legacy original without the marker remains unknown and generic state writes cannot backfill it. One policy-only helper shapes caller snapshots and both owning receivers without granting unit-seed or checkpoint authority. | `[unit]` `src/core/runLedger/checkpointState.test.ts::original session policy custody::*`; `::original policy owning state transaction::*`; `::matches omitted-policy caller bytes to actual InMemory readback after accepted reply loss`; `deploy/cloudflare-memory/runLedger.test.ts::original policy owning SQLite state transaction::*`; `::matches omitted-policy caller bytes to actual SQLite readback after accepted HTTP reply loss` |

| Criterion | Proof |
|---|---|
| An explicit versioned original workspace allocation is validated against canonical admission identity and retained through promotion, reclaim, finish and abandon. Missing legacy authority cannot be retrofitted; caller mutation, generic state/record writes and replacement metadata cannot create disposal authority. | `[unit]` `src/core/runLedger/workspaceDurability.test.ts::original workspace allocation::*` |
| New original cold allocation data uses a closed version 2 contract binding exact original run/requester/thread/repository/ref/initial head and positive PR number to the recorded registered purpose, retained-resident placement and exclusive-cold custody clause. Names, read-only labels and raw flags cannot construct it; missing, contradictory or mismatched data refuses construction. | Pull-owner lookup and admission use versioned, validated terminal candidate keys for the exact repository, PR and normalized branch, then read canonical candidates plus all live/unit/effect/workspace and audit dependencies in the owning transaction. Source inserts, updates and deletions invalidate the SQLite index atomically; the in-memory store compares current source facts. Bounded backfill makes idempotent derived progress without proving absence until all sources are covered. Missing/dirty/incomplete coverage, candidate content that differs from its validated footprint and unreadable global or legacy producer facts remain held. A derived-cache mismatch invalidates only that cache; a later bounded lookup must reread canonical source. Candidate rows retain the existing row/byte bounds; an index key grants no owner, transfer, source confirmation or execution authority. Canonical history, unknown writes, budgets and source holds remain unchanged. | `[unit]` `src/core/coordinator/pullOwnerIndex.test.ts::bounded terminal pull-owner candidates::*`; `deploy/cloudflare-memory/runLedger.test.ts::bounded SQLite terminal pull-owner candidates::*`; `src/core/coordinator/pullOwnership.test.ts::complete canonical pull ownership::*`; `deploy/cloudflare-memory/runLedger.test.ts::unit effect owner transaction::*` |
| 50: one bounded current effect lives on the existing unit; admission reads exact whole unit, caller Workflow/action, stop state and complete canonical target owners in the same owner transaction. Pending is persisted before a call; stop-first denies new calls, a begun call retains its accepted outcome after stop, and unknown/lost-response work cannot replay or retire. Exact branch-ref observations may resolve pending/unknown branch creation to its frozen commit without another POST. Missing, moved or foreign observations cannot resolve it. Merge observations match the complete frozen PR target and native commit; accepted queue effects cannot settle generically and retain ownership until exact terminal merge or known-entry absence. Unknown enqueue cannot resolve from absence. Operation-specific receipt shapes are enforced by the decoder, owner planner and HTTP matcher. Cancellation records only a never-started call; ordinary CAS and batches cannot introduce or erase effect authority. Admission and ordinary active-cell writes reserve maximum receipt bytes alongside the existing recovery settlement allowance | `[unit]` `src/core/coordinator/unitEffect.test.ts::unit current effect::*`, `deploy/cloudflare-memory/runLedger.test.ts::unit effect owner transaction::*`, `src/core/coordinator/recoveryHistory.test.ts::recovery history store::effect admission retains recovery settlement space…` |

| 35: a final answer or review verdict retries one unavailable checkpoint state acknowledgment on the same owned run. If the answer's transcript is durable but the state acknowledgment remains unavailable, the run retains its live row for a later generation to finish from the saved turns; no answer or review is published before recovery. The adopted run rebuilds a missing cursor from the verified whole transcript. The domain reader restores only the accepted result of the latest review submission. A later rejected or uncertain submission leaves earlier accepted facts in the transcript, without a recoverable current verdict. The run loop still retains a stored verdict when no accepted transcript verdict exists; `[gap]` finalization must distinguish that historical value from an incomplete current review before publication. A missing or broken cursor, ownership fence, permanent state refusal or unknown state error still fails closed. ### Coordinator producer identity

Canonical spawn requires the original unit, executing Workflow and recovery action, exact step/preset and monotone effect ordinal. It persists pending before dispatch and acknowledges only the exact durable child receipt. Retry uses listings to locate candidates; listings do not authorize adoption or prove absence. An unknown dispatch remains retryable for observation and cannot execute twice. Branch reuse leaves the ordinal unchanged; creating a branch and dispatching a child each consume one ordinal.


| Criterion | Proof |
|---|---|
| A child registration notification follows the committed admission identity and setup stream, including exactly one coordinator tag. | `src/core/dispatcher.test.ts::run ledger write-through (docs/reference/specs/run-history.md item 35)::coordinator registration waits for the durable identity and exact setup tag before notifying its owner: …` |
| An attaching restart retains the original durable stream, canonical publication, admitted profile and absolute allowance. Resident wait bounds cannot exceed that allowance. Missing or conflicting publication evidence cannot become a new review target. | `src/core/boot.test.ts::reclaimRuns::a row reserved at admission…`, `src/core/resumeLaunch.test.ts::the pure pieces::repoContextOf restores the coordinator review base from its durable admission tag`, `src/core/dispatcher.test.ts::run ledger write-through (docs/reference/specs/run-history.md item 35)::coordinator producer identity::a restarted coordinator review retains its original publication before resolving a moved head`, `::a canonical child retains its original profile and absolute budget through…` |
| Exact finished child evidence resolves a lost dispatch acknowledgement without dispatch replay; unproven registry/history candidates and uncertain outcomes leave queued attachments unconsumed. | `src/channels/adminCoordinator.test.ts::spawn durable effect admission::a lost dispatch reply resolves from the exact finished review without dispatching it again`, `src/channels/adminCoordinator.test.ts::the fold — a unit's thread events reach the pipeline's next step (record 0051's fold rule)::an uncertain coding dispatch leaves its attachment unconsumed and never replays on retry`, `::a registry-only spawn candidate cannot authorize adoption or consume queued thread events` |

| A coordinator child reserves its same ID before registry creation, then installs the shared setup finalizer immediately after creation and before its link or acknowledgement exposes the ID. A reservation failure returns a typed non-200 without an id. Post-ack pre-model failure retains the original parent, key, thread, repository and ref with a typed nonempty reason; replay creates no child or budget. Foreign and missing reads remain indistinguishable. | `[unit]` `src/core/dispatcher.test.ts::run ledger write-through (docs/reference/specs/run-history.md item 35)::coordinator producer identity::*` |
| The shared setup finalizer remains active until durable promotion and pre-loop setup succeed. Ordinary pre-effect setup failures retain the existing failed-child and workspace-release behavior. Unknown accepted original promotion or session ownership retains the original row, binding and held context without finalizing, abandoning, releasing, seeding or admitting the model. ### Historical recovery evidence

The internal `recoveryEvidence` listing is an admission audit, not a UI page. It selects the union of the original parent instance, an exact same-unit idempotency-key prefix or bare `<instance>:<unit>` key (even with a contradictory parent), and the original coding/review threads before applying the unchanged 200-row bound. No user, repository, thread-only or cursor filter may narrow this query. Ordinary run-list paging remains unchanged. The query proves completeness within retained history only; it does not reconstruct missing records, prices, caps, or lease origins.

| Criterion | Proof |
|---|---|
| The local stores and SQLite query select either identity claim and original threads before the cap; the bare same-unit key is retained for rejection by the child audit, while adjacent units do not match. More than 200 unrelated retained runs do not consume the evidence bound. SQL attests completeness only for a short valid result; full relevant sets, malformed candidate JSON and narrowed/cursor queries cannot attest it. | `[unit]` `src/core/runStore.test.ts::* — RunStore contract::recovery evidence selects either identity claim and original threads before the cap`; `deploy/cloudflare-memory/runs.test.ts::run history routes::recovery evidence filters before the cap and attests only a complete valid set` |
| Recovery attestation validates every retained summary with the record parser before trusting SQL identity selection. Valid JSON that is not a run record (scalars, arrays, missing required fields or malformed optional fields) cannot hide a foreign-thread child; any unreadable retained summary refuses completeness, including a pinned row beyond a maxRuns gap or an older pinned source outside the age cutoff. Unreadable existing records remain protected beyond ordinary retention limits and refuse completeness. Validation streams over retained evidence without changing the relevant-row cap. | `[unit]` `deploy/cloudflare-memory/runs.test.ts::run history routes::recovery evidence refuses a retained foreign-thread summary that is*`, `::recovery evidence refuses unreadable protected bytes beyond ordinary retention limits`, `::recovery evidence refuses a malformed context-pinned row beyond a retention gap`, `::recovery evidence refuses a malformed pinned source older than the age cutoff` |
| The Worker client requires the explicit completeness attestation, no cursor, a short bounded set of valid relevant rows and no duplicate ids. An old Worker without this contract cannot accidentally authorize recovery. | `[unit]` `src/core/runStoreWorker.test.ts::WorkerRunStore::recovery evidence requires an explicit complete bounded response without malformed or cursor rows` |
| Admission bypasses the UI ledger cache and cap, reads live evidence before history, and preserves conflicting source identities. ## Ordinary answer outcomes

The dispatcher records `AnswerOutcome` before fallback text and Markdown rendering. Its typed ending and authored-output presence are independent of verification, audience refusal, and `replyOk`. A tracked run commits those facts before entering `finishing`; an unavailable write prevents delivery and an ownership fence prevents both delivery and record replacement. Recovered state uses the same strict decoder as `RunRecord`. Missing or malformed legacy facts cannot become present output from prose. A completed execution status is not proof of requested-work completion.

`finishing` fences replay before channel delivery. Reclaim preserves the saved outcome but leaves delivery unknown; its card stays amber and says delivery was not confirmed. A general or research budget close with absent output explicitly says the answer was not written, ahead of the advisory checklist so adapter clipping cannot hide it. Continuation and authorized run readers consume the same saved outcome. A nonempty partial write-up establishes authored text only.

| Criterion | Proof |
| --- | --- |
| The producer captures empty and present raw write-ups independently of their claims; legacy, malformed and future data cannot supply facts | `[unit]` `src/core/answerOutcome.test.ts::ordinary answer outcome::*` |
| Delivery waits for the saved result; an unavailable write and an ownership fence suppress publication; failed or withheld delivery never rewrites authored output | `[unit]` `src/core/dispatch/reply.test.ts::deliverAnswer — the answer reaches the thread::*` |
| Reclaim before the final seal retains the saved missing answer with delivery unknown | `[unit]` `src/core/boot.test.ts::reclaimRuns::a restart before the final seal retains the missing write-up without claiming delivery` |
| Stored and recovered outcomes share validation and remain independent of delivery | `[unit]` `src/core/dispatch/record.test.ts::assembleRunRecord — the handoff on the record::validates producer outcome separately from fallback text and delivery` |

## Canonical pull ownership lookup

Ownership admission diagnostics identify the failing validation check, row source and index, and bounded scan counts. The state Worker logs these facts with the attempted instance and unit. It never logs row contents, publication text or private evidence. Diagnostics leave the ownership result and retained bytes unchanged.

## Restart identity capture paths

| Criterion | Proof |
|---|---|
| Refused ownership admission reports its failing check and bounded row location without exposing private contents or changing the refusal or retained rows. Incomplete unit-write replies carry the existing closed read-diagnostic shape through the authenticated Worker client. Unknown or private extra fields are omitted; reason-only replies remain compatible. | `[unit]` `src/core/coordinator/pullOwnership.test.ts::pull ownership refusal diagnostics::*`, `deploy/cloudflare-memory/runLedger.test.ts::coordinator admission diagnostics::*`; `src/core/coordinator/instanceStore.test.ts::pull-owner diagnostic reader::preserves only a closed diagnostic on an incomplete unit write` |

## Final admitted profile

A finished run retained in the live registry exposes its admitted `profile` from the persisted record, just as the cold stored read does. A missing record or a legacy record without a profile remains unknown; this adds metadata behind the existing authorized run read, not an authority route.

Malformed owner identity yields an unbound dependency observation, never invented empty ownership. Unsafe/live source keys, other refusal stages, malformed/foreign payloads and hash failures yield no locator. Default and anonymous results, authentication, scan bounds, source bytes, owner/admission/Review gates remain unchanged. A stale/missing locator gives no absence, retry, release, ending, continuation or ACK authority; named readbacks must match the snapshot. ## Terminal completion and recovery

An acknowledged `ended` assignment commits its event, terminal result and `finishing` phase in the same ledger transaction. Execution cannot resume after this boundary. Channel delivery and workspace settlement remain separate obligations; completion does not prove either one. A same-owner `finishing` call confirms this existing boundary, while a former owner stays fenced. Recovery settles older ended rows without starting another model run, preserves their terminal status and completion time, and leaves delivery unknown without a receipt.

## The run's setup result

A run that asked for a workspace records its **setup result** (`RunRecord.setup`, [record 0097](../../decisions/0097-every-run-row-carries-its-outcome-and-workspace-and-the-review-dashboard-is-six-queries-over-them.md)): what its repository and profile requested (`resident`, `cold` or `blank`), and, when it got something else, a fallback word (a sandbox stood in for a resident, with how the sandbox was filled) or a lost word (no workspace). The words are in `src/core/runSetup.ts`. The factory decides a fresh selection's result from its own branch and the resident's typed refusal code, never from the note (`ExecutorSelection.setup`). The claim writes it on the row beside the binding, so it is durable before the first model turn; a same-id restart's claim marks it `reallocated`. A failed attach names a lost word from the error's type (`lostWordOf` in `src/core/dispatch/provision.ts`) on the setup failure record. A resumed run keeps the result its row was claimed with, and a reclaimed row closes with it. One strict decoder (`runSetupOf`) reads the row, the reclaimed state and the stored record; a shape whose fields disagree is dropped, never stored. A run that asked for no workspace has no setup result. On a local or E2B deployment the result also names its `backend`, so it never reads as a Cloudflare sandbox. Beside it, `RunRecord.targetPr` carries the pull request number the run was admitted to act on (the repository context's, before the model starts; the reclaimed row's `meta.pr`), distinct from `pr`, the one a coding post-step opened.

| Criterion | Proof |
| --- | --- |

## Storage failure telemetry

One shared contract owns storage failure evidence: the digest-bound header and closed server failure validator, `storageFailureNotice`, the `storage_failure` run event, and the existing privacy and retention rules. Exception and validation-rejection producers use the same Worker log emitter. The state-only header is a compatibility alias; state RPC guards and recovery remain operation-specific and never broaden replay authority.

Storage diagnostics describe observed failures. They never acknowledge a mutation, permit replay, or grant recovery or completion credit. The shared ledger request boundary captures reads and writes before an action-specific handler can swallow the exception. Request identity and digests come from the frozen wire bytes. Redacted exceptions carry bounded stacks, causes and platform flags; request and response bodies remain excluded. Decoder exceptions that can quote response bytes are excluded. Operator logs remain available when event storage is unavailable or the run has already ended. Failed diagnostic-only appends log without generating another diagnostic event.

First-party run storage transports use a closed operation identity contract. Each operation names its exact run targets or an explicit non-run scope. Required targets are part of the typed call; missing or contradictory target metadata is refused before dispatch. Identifier strings admitted by broader legacy protocols remain explicitly unresolved, and existing owning validators remain authoritative. Session keys, cursors, coordinator history and unrelated run-like fields do not supply targets. External requests with unknown or malformed target identity remain explicitly unresolved. The Worker derives identity from its authenticated parsed request under the same operation contract. Existing route validation, ownership and acknowledgement rules still apply.

The request witness captures target primitives, diagnostic-only append status and one request ID before transport. A later failure does not decode request bytes. Encoded requests must match the supplied operation identity before dispatch; serialized identity is checked once at admission. Client and Worker failures retain the same request ID and exact wire digest. The immutable notice and observer retain every batch target. Operator output uses one bounded projection with at most 200 target IDs and an aggregate byte budget. It records total, emitted and omitted target counts; target IDs are not duplicated inside the log context. Omitted exception details are explicit. This does not guarantee platform log retention or capture of every invocation. Diagnostic capture does not change mutation retry policy.

The request boundary owns the primary client failure log. Saved error metadata does not prove that this log was emitted. The reporting helper records that observation only after the primary logger returns and before it calls the observer. Replacing boundary metadata clears any earlier reporting observation, including when a caller reuses an error or the observer replaces its metadata. A fallback warning or observer callback does not establish it. Domain wrappers inherit the original metadata and reporting observation without another primary log. The observer still receives the full notice if logging fails. Service outcomes can use this observation to avoid a duplicate failure log while retaining their own recovery result.

The transport and ledger boundaries clear old error evidence before they build a failure notice. If capture fails, they keep the original storage error and emit `storage.request.failure.unavailable` with the frozen request identity and bounded target counts. This fallback does not parse payload bytes, clone diagnostic data or include exception text. Full metadata remains unavailable, and the error remains unreported for service fallback. The unavailable warning does not confirm that the primary failure was reported.

Client failures also retain elapsed time, the configured HTTP deadline and whether that deadline signal fired. A fresh transport request ID distinguishes identical wire payloads. State, heartbeat, append and step RPC progress logs join that ID to the exact digest, original run, owner generation, serving build, stage and attempt. Each call logs before awaiting RPC and after completion or failure. A completed RPC is an observed return, not a mutation receipt. Missing completion logs leave the outcome unknown; retention or sampling gaps do not prove a call never reached the Worker. These observations do not authorize HTTP mutation replay. Append, heartbeat and step retry only their original fenced, replay-safe RPC, with a fresh stub and the shared 200 ms and 800 ms waits. They stop after three attempts or when the remaining HTTP deadline cannot cover the next wait. Exhaustion preserves the original error and leaves unconfirmed writes unknown.

The pure `/runs/state` guard read has a 2-second attempt bound and uses the same 200 ms and 800 ms backoffs, with at most three attempts and a fresh stub each time. The original HTTP budget starts before body consumption and reserves 2 seconds for the original state mutation. Exhaustion, request cancellation and insufficient remaining time stop admission to that mutation. A late guard reply cannot replace the accepted baseline or start another write. The route releases each RPC result's owned resources on return, failure, timeout or abort; release does not prove remote cancellation. Pending mutations and heartbeats do not acquire a deadline-based replay rule. Guard and heartbeat receiver progress joins the validated request ID, exact digest, run, generation and attempt to the object ID, incarnation, closed stages, duration and safe error name. A receiver return is not proof that the caller received it. Missing logs still do not prove absence or a provider-internal cause.

State and heartbeat requests also carry positive progress observations. An authenticated POST records receipt before body consumption with a valid caller request ID when present. A decoded record joins the existing exact-body witness to valid target identity before the first RPC; invalid JSON has no decoded record. The client records accepted or refused only after its existing acknowledgment envelope validator succeeds. HTTP headers alone, a malformed or interrupted body, and an unknown acknowledgment cannot produce acceptance. A missing caller ID stays absent at receipt and cannot be joined to a later generated ID. Progress logging adds no network calls, storage writes or retries. Each record is bounded to 1 KiB, and a failed primary log attempts only one bounded fallback warning. Both sink failures preserve the original result. Missing observations prove neither absence, provider queueing nor reply loss. The [design record](../../plans/storage-request-observations.md) states the provider tracing and live privacy boundaries.

The base-config and runtime-override clients use the same failure notice for `/config/get` and `/config/put`. Their request start time is distinct from the time a later report is saved. Elapsed time and deadline state are frozen at the failure boundary before asynchronous diagnostic work. Response metadata uses closed content-type and observed-body-format values; an unreadable or unread body is explicit. HTML or JSON alone does not prove whether an edge service or the Worker answered. Bodies, arbitrary headers, document keys and credentials stay out of notices. A diagnostic sink or request-ID failure cannot change the original result or cause a retry.

The shared response decoder retains only the closed body-format tag against the original response. Diagnostics use that tag without reading the body again. An externally consumed response without decoder evidence is `unknown`, not `not-read`. Runtime overrides also report an unreadable successful response while keeping their existing empty-document result.

| Criterion | Proof |
| --- | --- |
| A confirmed Cloudflare internal error names Cloudflare's infrastructure boundary and retains its support reference on new storage notes and retained timelines. A generic HTTP 500 or an application message that quotes the platform text does not assign Cloudflare ownership. | `[unit]` `src/core/storageFailureDiagnostic.test.ts::storage diagnostic captures::names Cloudflare's boundary on new and retained storage failures without blaming a generic 500` |
| Append, heartbeat and step requests retry only their original fenced, replay-safe Durable Object RPC after a retryable platform failure, with waits of 200 ms and 800 ms and at most three attempts. Each attempt gets a fresh stub. Overloads, explicit non-retryable errors and application errors stop immediately. An exhausted request remains failed; no HTTP mutation is replayed and no work is re-executed. | `[unit]` `deploy/cloudflare-memory/runLedger.test.ts::state-write holds across Durable Object RPC::recovers Cloudflare RPC failure*`; `::bounds Cloudflare RPC retries*` |

## Private recorded check custody

Final close, hard-stop completion and unclaimed restart closure retain bounded canonical checks and the full authorization resource from the fenced live row in private evidence. Submitted history cannot mint or replace it. Restart snapshots remain unsealed, the next owner preserves original call identities, and final close captures its latest state. Malformed checks and combined record/evidence byte overflow refuse before deleting live evidence. Public run projections do not gain check output. Existing history retention applies; missing source remains unavailable.

|---|---|
| Final capture uses canonical private authority, refuses caller archives and malformed source, and replaces an unsealed restart snapshot with the final owner state. | `[unit]` `src/core/runLedger/checkInspection.test.ts::canonical check terminal custody::*`; `src/core/runLedger/inMemory.test.ts::cancelled check terminal custody::*`; `[unit]` `deploy/cloudflare-memory/runLedger.test.ts::recorded check terminal custody in real SQLite::*`; `deploy/cloudflare-memory/runLedger.test.ts::cancelled check terminal custody in real SQLite::*` |
| Provisional puts never freeze an early check snapshot. Actual UTF-8 private evidence is counted with the record; overflow refuses without finishing or deleting original unknown evidence. | `[unit]` `deploy/cloudflare-memory/runLedger.test.ts::recorded check terminal custody in real SQLite::*`; `deploy/cloudflare-memory/runLedger.test.ts::cancelled check terminal custody in real SQLite::*` |

| Abandon only removes an unstarted reservation. Any recorded command, preflight or malformed check evidence refuses before deleting the original live row; absent or strictly empty check state remains allowed. | `[unit]` `src/core/runLedger/inMemory.test.ts::recorded check abandon custody::*`; `deploy/cloudflare-memory/runLedger.test.ts::recorded check abandon custody in real SQLite::*` |

## PR identity belongs to work

A conversation can contain several work items and PRs, including PRs in different repositories. The existing unit row retains its PR relation after a run ends. Active publication ownership can end separately. Each unit binds at most one PR number across its PR, resume, publication and adoption fields. A write cannot change that identity by retaining the old number in a different field. Legacy rows remain readable; contradictory target changes refuse. A fresh unit can bind its first PR through the existing accepted native publication path.

## Original managed review publication custody

The canonical review journal records the original native POST target. A pending or uncertain managed review remains attributable to its original run and unit after that unit ends. A new head, terminal status or another request does not settle the publication. Owner reads retain this original obligation before a new managed effect or sibling work can be admitted. Observation and settlement of the original unit effect still use its existing fence. Accepted, refused and absent journals release this publication obligation; other active unit, effect and workspace obligations remain independent.

The terminal candidate index routes a review by repository and PR. A conservative repository candidate also loads it for ref-only requests; the canonical original unit determines whether that exact ref is held. The index version changes with the projection, and old coverage is rebuilt from stored journals within the existing limits. Invalid managed receipts remain incomplete before target filtering. No private transcript or review body enters this projection.

## Shared source-event reception

The existing ledger accepts bounded source data before a producer can be enabled. An event has separate source, provider delivery, native object and change IDs. Its kind is a request, source evidence or a typed source fact. The event contains no trusted worker destination or requester grant. Receipt sequence numbers describe local reception order, not upstream completeness.

The state worker saves the event, its first pending job and the next shared alarm inside one asynchronous SQLite storage transaction. The reference ledger provides the same reception and replay results. A receipt confirms reception only. It does not confirm relevance, routing, internal delivery, consumption or an external effect.

Repeated delivery returns the original receipt before checking queue capacity or decoding the job. It cannot reset a completed or unknown future job. Different content under the same source and delivery ID is refused; a content hash does not determine event order. An unreadable stored event or job is held, not treated as absent.

Reception permits at most 400 KiB of canonical event JSON, 1,024 retained events and 32 MiB of retained event JSON per existing ledger partition. These are provisional limits for an inactive producer. A due job contributes a 30-second recovery wake to the existing shared alarm; an earlier shared deadline remains earlier. Source rows are independent of run ending and run cleanup. This cut has no source producer, job claim, execution loop or event-retention policy. Those must be qualified before enablement.

## Source job claims and retry

A compatible consumer can claim at most 16 saved source jobs in one request. A claim grants a 60-second queue lease with a server-generated token. It grants no task, tool, source-read or publication authority and cannot renew an agent's execution budget. A stopped claimant leaves the input saved; after expiry another process can claim it with a new token and attempt number.

Each claim request scans at most 64 eligible records. The saved scan position advances past unsupported records without changing them, so those records cannot permanently hide later work. An incompatible reader cannot claim or change a job. The version 2 job reader also accepts original version 1 received jobs. Reader version 3 also understands saved relevance records. Version 2 callers hold version 3 jobs without consuming attempts; no reader downgrades retained records.

Retry and hold are separate outcomes. Temporary source, storage, provider or context unavailability can request a bounded retry delay. A permanent permission refusal, unsupported schema or invalid source can only hold the job as failed. Eight attempts exhaust automatic recovery. Failure retains the original source and a closed failure reason; it proves neither delivery nor absence of an external effect.

A release receipt is retained under its claim token. Repeating the same release returns its original result before checking the current lease, even if a later process has claimed the job. Changed release metadata under that token is refused. Stale or expired tokens cannot change another claim. Claim/retry state and the scheduled wake commit in the same storage transaction. Unknown HTTP acknowledgments remain uncertain and have no automatic retry or fresh-work fallback.

No source processor, producer or job completion path is enabled by this capability. Completion still requires the later internal-delivery contract. A lost claim response can leave a lease held until expiry; it cannot erase input. The client does not retry that claim implicitly. Structured HTTP outcome logs contain counts, opaque event keys and failure reasons, without source text or lease tokens.

## Keyed inbox delivery receipts

The existing ledger has an inactive `deliverInbox` protocol for an internal caller that already has delivery authority. A request names a version, stable delivery key and frozen message. It does not grant permissions, select a task or start a fresh run. The caller must save its decision and exact message before it uses this protocol.

One SQLite transaction writes the existing inbox row and a receipt with the run ID, delivery key, body hash and sequence number. A retry of the same run/key/body returns the original receipt, even if the target has ended. Changed input under the same key is refused. Receipt lookup precedes live-target and capacity checks. Receipt rows survive run closure and history deletion; there is no expiry that silently allows another append. Their retirement still needs a separate lifetime and consumption contract before activation.

A run in `finishing` refuses a new keyed delivery with `not-live`, including when it entered that phase while the input was being hashed. The SQLite check and append share the delivery transaction. This refusal means the run no longer accepts new keyed input; it does not prove that its live row has disappeared. An exact saved delivery still replays its receipt, and changed input under that key still conflicts. Handoff rows remain eligible for queued input. The ordinary requester inbox path keeps its existing settlement behavior.

Each run retains at most 256 keyed receipts. New keys at that limit are held; existing receipts can still be read by retry. Each message uses the existing 400 KiB inbox bound. The store captures canonical JSON before hashing, rejects supplied custody and stamps the existing destination custody on append. Unsupported receipt versions remain held. A failed receipt insert rolls back the inbox row in the same transaction.

The HTTP client accepts only a receipt for its exact run, key and body hash. Missing routes, unreadable acknowledgments and ambiguous transport results do not fall back to the ordinary inbox route. Deploy compatible readers before any caller enables the protocol. Current dispatch and steer writers still use their existing path. This receipt proves saved delivery, not model consumption; exact consumption and source-job settlement remain pending.

### Link delivery to an observed row

The delivery hash covers canonical message data before the store stamps destination custody. The inbox row witness hashes the complete stored message, including custody. The hashes therefore cover different data. `inboxDeliveryWitness` checks both hashes, run and sequence IDs, the known custody schema, and the expected channel, thread and requester. It removes only the store-owned `target` field when reconstructing the delivery hash. Captured row data keeps its JSON property order through asynchronous hashing.

The caller must obtain the delivery receipt through the ledger's verified response. This helper correlates supplied data; it does not authenticate the delivery key, certify first-claim identity or grant current execution authority. An observed witness is still not native consumption. No delivery caller, consumption ACK, retirement path or source producer is enabled by this helper.

## Bounded original-ingress metadata

A valid completed ingress owner retires after its original receipt/origin horizon only when no live, provisional, restarting, unresolved custody or retained dependency requires it. Ordinary messages, target answers and saved confirmations use the same rule. Retirement removes original attribution, the run lookup index and its deletion marker together; an expired original request cannot create a new owner. Missing horizons, invalid records and unproved custody remain protected. This removes no run, transcript, model, workspace or canonical answer. A canonical terminal machine:none record may release only this metadata when its exact owner and first-claim identity match, its answer ending is known, and no live, provisional, restarting, promotion, physical-allocation contradiction or pending custody remains. A null allocation alone supplies no ending or physical cleanup authority. A retained native media consumer keeps its exact A/B source owners until that consumer leaves policy; missing or unknown ending/profile and corrupt custody retain them.

Deletion-before-consume stores a finite deadline from protected receipt/config metadata on the existing clear operation. Known origin deadlines and existing marker deadlines never move on retries or configuration changes. Legacy key-only markers receive one baseline on upgraded access; corrupt associated ownership remains protected. Older internal callers use the declared receipt default. The existing alarm and ingress operations retire bounded rotating batches; no separate controller is introduced.

Legacy live and finished run context is backfilled once through a fixed run-ID snapshot and durable cursor in the existing metadata table. Each pass selects at most 100 distinct run IDs, with one small ID lookahead. A run can have both live and finished rows, so it reads at most 200 table bodies, one at a time. No original-ingress owner retires until that census completes. Corrupt census addresses or required context hold retirement; later passes and a reconstructed Worker continue the same snapshot. Current accepted claim/state/finish and thread-session writes maintain the same exact A/B pins, including run IDs behind the migration cursor. Session roots use their existing indexed registration; unit roots retain their existing handoff/ancestor indexes, without a cross-store history reader.

The additive clear-protection fields require compatible Memory handling for bounded retirement. Older Memory still protects deletion, but cannot prove the new metadata expiry or native leaf accounting. Compatible Memory must precede native v3 writer activation; Memory rollback below that floor is unsafe once those leaves exist. Existing bot context readers must retain v3 support. Legacy session context cannot be reconstructed from the run-store metadata table. Publication, deployment and this minimum-reader qualification remain separate owner-controlled gates.

## Typed source evidence message

The shared inbox codec has a versioned `source-evidence` message. It retains source, delivery, object and change IDs; a nullable native author; the original source receipt and timestamps; source text; and an orchestrator run/decision reference. Source text and author fields carry no requester, credential, private-audience, approval or control authority. Even evidence authored by the requester remains evidence. Empty text and unknown authors remain explicit source data.

The decoder checks the known schema, source receipt key and decision-ID shape. A stored row also needs the expected run, channel, thread and requester custody. Its producer generation remains original through reclaim. Wire messages cannot supply custody. The message uses the existing 400 KiB inbox bound and is never truncated. Unknown schemas and unsafe JSON remain held.

This validates data consistency. It does not authenticate the source receipt or decision. A consumer must read their canonical records, verify source metadata and content, and check current destination authority before delivery. Current runtimes hold this kind instead of interpreting it as a requester message. A present non-message tag also remains held when its version is missing; it cannot use the legacy requester decoder.

Version 2 retains the event's common content and distinguishes a first `notice` from an internal `fold`. Both name the original orchestrator owner. A notice has no prior fold decision. A fold requires a decision reference from that owner. Text state, exact text (including empty or null), metadata and typed facts remain data. Stored notices must match the expected root's four custody coordinates; this is consistency proof, not proof that the stored run is an orchestrator.

`sourceEvidenceMessageFromRecord` projects only version 2 evidence/fact records whose complete event matches their source receipt. It never parses an opaque provider payload or upgrades a version 1 receipt. It captures the record and routing before asynchronous validation. The caller still must load canonical storage and check the actual root, decision, task context and current source access. Neither the codec nor this projection authenticates a caller-supplied record or route.

No producer or runtime reader is enabled. Canonical decision admission, source-job handling stages, typed delivery through both runtimes and unit events, exact native admission and checkpoint acknowledgment, and reader migration remain `[gap]`. Original source records and inbox bytes stay retained.

## Source input dependencies

Version 4 of the cumulative context envelope can retain an exact source-input reference. Each reference keeps the complete source receipt, original orchestrator coordinates, typed notice or fold, complete input hash and adapter source-identity hash. A fold also keeps its original owned decision ID and hash. These are data to validate, not requester credentials, permission, a delivery target or native input acknowledgment. Unsupported versions, changed required fields and imported authority fields stay refused; older envelope versions cannot carry these references.

Merges preserve source references, detect conflicting proof under the same original input identity, and apply the existing count and byte bounds. Version 4 reserves space for an explicit refusal state. The final normalized record is checked after its revision changes. If a new union exceeds capacity, it stays unknown and preserves the previous accepted context instead of discarding an older source. Checkpoint hashing and normalization retain the references. The existing retention index includes source roots from the saved child context and every flattened ancestor, as direct links from the actual consumer. It pins each original root run while that consumer remains a policy root, without resurrecting deleted data or making pinned runs independent retention roots.

The source-input consistency reader reconstructs the common message from the canonical source event and the original live orchestrator root. It checks the complete receipt and input hash. A fold also requires the exact owned decision, receipt and input. After hashing, the reader compares the source body again and reloads the root's complete current task and selected decision. It returns a fixed hold reason if proof is missing, changes or cannot be read. An archived root requires its exact retained `sourceTask` snapshot. The reader never reconstructs missing task fields from the archive's output head or other display metadata. Legacy archives without that snapshot stay held. A malformed, ambiguous or unsafe live root cannot fall back to an older valid archive.

The existing context-access reader now uses this consistency reader before model use. An optional adapter check runs inside validation, before the final source and task reread. The common guard checks the original root audience and current destination around that check. A missing verifier, unsupported storage reader, changed task or refused access retains `source_input_access_unproved`. The GitHub adapter binds a versioned hash of the complete original task and native source identity. It reuses current App access, the requester repository grant and, for private repositories, the bound person and verified direct audience. A changed identity hash is held; the hash itself grants no permission. No permission result is cached across model calls.

These checks also protect context derived from source input, including summaries and saved checkpoints. They do not deliver input or prove native input acknowledgment. Per-event obligations, pending-input retention, exact native acknowledgment, compatible reader qualification and producer activation remain separate gates. Source producers and delivery callers remain disabled.

### Original native input intent

An input intent records the host-assigned ID for one source inbox row before native submission. `sourceInputIntents` keeps that ID, original run and owner generation, requester and destination, native session and launch ordinal, exact row witness, source reference and rendered-input hash in existing live run state. `prepared` can advance to `begun`; neither phase proves that the native process accepted input. A later native receipt must also retain Pi's generated entry ID or OpenCode's original input identity.

Only the current owner-fenced state writer can add a prepared intent or advance its phase, and only against previously saved matching native session facts and a begun launch. Ordinary state updates preserve all original entries. Reclaim changes the current writer, not an input's original generation. Unsupported, unsafe, oversized or conflicting data is refused rather than replaced or truncated. The archive task guard restores only its task field; the live-state guard checks input intents against the complete current state.

Each intent refusal reports one warning with the canonical run ID when available and a closed guard reason. The warning includes no input metadata, source text, native session or exception details. Valid writes and state without input intents produce no warning.

This is a live-state storage contract. It does not validate source permission or the canonical row digest, submit native input, acknowledge consumption, retain pending input after terminal finish, or activate delivery. No production caller creates these intents yet. Those paths must be qualified together before enabling a producer.

| The owning in-memory store binds first preparation to the current owner and previously saved native session; reclaim preserves original identity and begun cannot roll back | `[unit]` `src/core/runLedger/checkpointState.test.ts::source input intent state transaction::*` |
