## Retained original source tasks

The optional version 1 `sourceTask` snapshot stores the original orchestrator coordinates and the same eight task fields as the live fold-target projection: repository, branch, base branch, input head, PR number, accepted operation-target data, native GitHub identity and record task key. These are data to compare, not a requester, credential, current permission or executable action target. The complete snapshot is bounded to 16 KiB; unsafe data, unknown fields and unsupported versions stay unproved.

A first snapshot must match the store's canonical original-root task and owner. Existing owner-fenced state writes preserve it when a caller omits it and refuse replacement. The tracked finish and terminal recovery copy the acknowledged state field, without substituting the final workspace head. The Memory Worker retains it in the existing private work-evidence archive and preserves it on ordinary archive rewrites. Normal listing and run-view projections omit it. Capacity failure retains the original live state instead of dropping proof.

A state acknowledgment proves only that metadata write. It is not native input admission, inbox consumption or model consumption. No source-admission caller produces this field yet. The field's reader, writer, private-view handling and original-root binding must be qualified together before a producer is enabled. There is no backfill from incomplete legacy archives.

## Saved source relevance

The existing source job can retain a relevance recommendation for an orchestrator conversation and task-context hash. The record carries the original conversation coordinates, exact classifier-input hash, result source, verdict, reason and store-assigned time. The source receipt binds the save to the original event. A context hash must cover the complete task snapshot; the input hash must cover the actual classifier input and policy. These are caller-supplied references, not a proof of source access, current context or an authorized decision.

Only a current queue owner can save a new result. A repeated result for the same context returns the original record before checking the current lease or capacity. Changed input or result under that context is refused. Different task snapshots and conversations have separate records. Each event holds at most 16 records; reasons have a 1,024-byte UTF-8 limit and each identity has a 512-character limit. Each result also has a 4-KiB JSON byte limit. These provisional limits bound inactive storage; enablement still requires measured capacity and retention.

Saving a result upgrades the existing job to version 3. Claim, retry, expiry and permanent failure preserve every saved result. Incompatible readers hold the job. The save does not change its queue deadline, alarm, execution lease or completion state. A failed transaction restores the prior job. An unknown acknowledgment remains uncertain; it cannot justify another model call or a routing fallback.

No source processor calls this operation. Recovery must read and match the saved source, context and input before reusing a result. A result cannot select a specialist, ignore evidence by itself, grant permission, prove internal delivery, settle a job or retire input. Those integration steps remain pending.

## Common content on saved source events

Version 2 of the existing `SourceEvent` adds provider-neutral `content`. Requests and source evidence carry text with a common `present`, `deleted` or `withdrawn` state; source facts carry a JSON facts object. A dismissed review maps to withdrawn evidence. Optional message metadata retains source labels and native locations as data, not requester credentials or execution targets. Common handling can read that lifecycle and display metadata without parsing GitHub fields. Null text is unknown text, not an empty message or permission to infer a body from the provider payload. The native `payload` remains opaque in common handling code. The adapter still owns source authentication and projection.

The existing canonical JSON, size bound and body hash cover the content. The delivery key is unchanged. An exact repeat returns the original receipt; changed content under the same delivery identity is refused. An older stored event cannot be rewritten merely to add version 2 content. Version 1 remains readable without invented common content, and a version 1 envelope cannot carry a version 2 field. Unsupported versions, mismatched text/fact kinds and extra authority fields in the content envelope are refused. Facts can describe controls or permissions as untrusted data; they cannot execute a control or grant a permission.

The inactive GitHub converters emit version 2. Human and bot comments, inline replies, submitted reviews and commit comments share text content. PR, repository, thread, check/status, ref and installation changes share fact content. Common content holds the normalized text or facts once; the opaque payload retains native data and source-specific relationships. No receiver calls these converters, and no source processor or producer is activated. A future producer must verify compatible readers. A future handler must hold legacy or unsupported content rather than parse a provider payload or silently downgrade it. Event content versions are separate from queue job/relevance versions.

## Destructive run removal

Kill requires confirmation from the original producer and any required workspace cleanup. A sealed stream, a saved terminal record or the absence of a workspace does not prove shutdown. An unconfirmed producer ending retains its diagnostic record and refuses deletion. A retained legacy cancellation with only a `no-workspace` disposition also remains unconfirmed and cannot authorize deletion.

Ordinary and setup records retain `runtimeShutdownConfirmed: true` only after the original terminal write and original cleanup acknowledge. An absent field means unknown, including on legacy and boot-reclaimed records. A later positive acknowledgement updates that same record and clears a pending `runtime_shutdown_unconfirmed` marker. An uncertain terminal write does not start that update. With history enabled, Kill requires the current saved proof or an acknowledged prepared cancellation. With history off, it checks the original local control. Losing a control, or retaining an old control for a newer saved segment, does not grant permission to delete.

The cleanup update compares the original terminal identity and events, then changes only its shutdown metadata. It cannot replace a newer segment under the same run ID, erase newer metadata or restore deleted history. The Worker checks the comparison and the absence of a live owner in one SQLite transaction through `/runs/shutdown`. Unsupported stores retain an unknown outcome; no full-record fallback or retry follows an uncertain acknowledgement.

Initial fitting reserves room for the later shutdown flag. The acknowledgement leaves the saved events unchanged and stays within `MAX_RECORD_BYTES`.

`runs stop <id>` ends the current turn and forces runtime cleanup. It retains the run, who requested the stop, and the final stopped result. `runs kill <id>` skips the graceful turn interrupt and removes the run from live and saved run views after runtime shutdown is confirmed. Kill also permits explicit removal of a finished run. Existing `runs.stop` calls with an explicit `mode: soft|hard` keep their original non-destructive behavior and response mode. The mode is optional for the new Stop behavior. Deploy the history Worker before the bot and bundled dashboard so deletion intent, fences and reads are available.

The history store keeps a separate run-ID deletion fence. It rejects later record writes and same-ID claims. Kill removes the run row, its saved events and the existing history references. It retains source deduplication, ownership and shutdown evidence that prevent replay. Shared session logs, other runs, memory, metrics, external output and backups are outside this row-removal contract.

| A Stop accepted after normal delivery starts replaces the pending answer with one stopped result. | `[unit]` `src/core/dispatch/reply.test.ts::deliverAnswer…::a late Stop replaces the pending…` |

The feed tracks foreign active rows and starts with an authorized snapshot so a deletion between page and feed cannot strand a row. | `[unit]` `src/core/runsService.test.ts::RunsService.getRun::shared Kill fences cached foreign ledger reads and listings`; `src/channels/liveView.test.ts::shared Kill removes a foreign row seeded by the active index`; `::an index feed reconciles a row killed between the page and the feed request`; `web/src/pages/runsIndex.test.ts::RunsIndexPage — the live feed::reconciles the actual seeded rows when Kill falls before feed attachment` |

| Criterion | Proof |
|---|---|
| A private-context recovery hold stays live with its reason, hides the obsolete working projection, and preserves the canonical runtime state. | `[unit]` `src/core/runsService.test.ts::RunsService with the run ledger — one registry across generations (run-history item 41)::shows a private context recovery hold instead of the stale working state` |

| An uncertain private-context hold keeps its exact original request and expected state outside the live producer set. Concurrent or repeated calls await that original. Only exact same-owner readback clears it; reconciliation starts no heartbeat, mutation replay or runtime. | `[unit]` `src/core/runLedger/writeThrough.test.ts::adopt — a reclaimed run continues under this generation (item 37)::retains an unknown original hold write until exact same-owner readback without replay` |

Original live-state writes can retain a transaction-bound result under the [write receipt contract](write-receipts.md). Missing receipts remain unknown and grant no replay authority.
